<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove a finite-dimensional central limit theorem away from equilibrium for a deterministic grand-canonical hard-sphere gas in three dimensions. The initial probability density is smooth, with spatially summable Gaussian velocity bounds on the density and its spatial gradient. The limit holds on every finite interval on which the classical Boltzmann solution has uniform Gaussian velocity decay. The empirical measure is centered by its exact microscopic expectation. Its fluctuations converge to the Gaussian solution of the linear fluctuating Boltzmann equation.

- **claim-finite-dimensional-fluctuations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Under [ass:kinetic](#ass:kinetic), the centered Gaussian generalized field $\zeta$ with weak equation $$\label{eq:fluctuating-equation} \mathrm d\zeta_t(\phi)=\zeta_t(A_t\phi)\,\mathrm dt+\mathrm dW_t(\phi)$$ is well defined by its backward mild form on a scale of tests with small Gaussian growth. Its initial field is independent of $W$, and $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E[\zeta_0(\phi)\zeta_0(\psi)] &=\int_{\mathbb R^6}f_0\phi\psi\,\mathrm dz,\label{eq:initial-covariance}\\ \mathbb E[W_t(\phi)W_s(\psi)] &=\int_0^{t\wedge s}C_r(\phi,\psi)\,\mathrm dr.\label{eq:noise-covariance} \end{aligned}$$ The Gaussian noise $W$ has independent increments. For every integer $m\ge1$, fixed times $t_1,\ldots,t_m\in[0,T]$, and real tests $\phi_1,\ldots,\phi_m\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^6)$, $$\label{eq:main-convergence} \bigl(\zeta_{t_1}^\epsilon(\phi_1),\ldots, \zeta_{t_m}^\epsilon(\phi_m)\bigr) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \bigl(\zeta_{t_1}(\phi_1),\ldots, \zeta_{t_m}(\phi_m)\bigr) \qquad(\epsilon\downarrow0).$$
  - Explanation: Exact-centered empirical observations converge jointly to the Gaussian generalized field on the prescribed regular kinetic interval. The initial field and collision noise are independent.
- **claim-initial-ensemble** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-initial)): For all sufficiently small $\epsilon$, the following assertions hold.* 1. *The factorial densities satisfy $$\label{eq:initial-factorial-upper} 0\le F_{h,0}^{\epsilon}\le\prod_{j=1}^h f_0(z_j), \qquad (1-C\epsilon)f_0\le g_0\le f_0.$$ Consequently $\\|g_0-f_0\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},2\beta}=O(\epsilon)$ and, for every fixed integer $p\ge1$, $$\label{eq:initial-N-moments} \mathbb E(N)_p\le\mu^p, \qquad \sup_{0<\epsilon\le\epsilon_0}\mathbb E(N/\mu)^p<\infty.$$* 2. *With $\upsilon=1/81$, for every $h\ge2$, $$\label{eq:initial-coarse-links} \|c_{h,0}^{\epsilon}(Z_h)\| \le (Ch)^{Ch}\prod_{j=1}^h f_0(z_j) \sum_{\mathcal T\text{ tree on }[h]} \prod_{\{i,j\}\in\mathcal T} \bigl(\mathbf 1_{\|x_i-x_j\|\le\epsilon}+\epsilon^\upsilon\bigr).$$ Here $C$ and the upper bound on $\epsilon$ are independent of $h$. For fixed $h\ge2$ and $D>0$ there is $C_{D,h}<\infty$ such that $$\label{eq:initial-sharp-links} \|c_{h,0}^{\epsilon}(Z_h)\| \le C_{D,h}\prod_{j=1}^h f_0(z_j) \sum_{\mathcal T\text{ tree on }[h]} \prod_{\{i,j\}\in\mathcal T} \bigl(\mathbf 1_{\|x_i-x_j\|\le C_{D,h}\epsilon}+\epsilon^D\bigr).$$ Moreover, $$\label{eq:initial-integrated-cumulants} \\|c_{h,0}^{\epsilon}\\|_{L^1}\le C_h\epsilon^{3(h-1)} \qquad(h\ge2).$$* 3. *For every finite list of bounded real tests, the exact-centered initial fluctuations converge jointly to a centered Gaussian vector with covariance $\int f_0\phi\psi$. For a smooth compactly supported real test $\phi$, one also has $$\label{eq:initial-centering-shift} \lim_{\epsilon\to0}\sqrt\mu \left(\mathbb E\pi_0^\epsilon(\phi)-\int f_0\phi\right) =-\frac{4\pi}{3}\int_{\mathbb R^6} f_0(x,v)\phi(x,v)\varrho_0(x)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv.$$
  - Explanation: The initial ensemble has controlled factorial moments and connected densities, a Gaussian initial fluctuation limit, and a generally nonzero excluded-volume correction to its mean.
- **claim-global-weighted-integrability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-target-moments)): For every $0<q<2\beta$, $$\label{eq:target-velocity-moments} \sup_{0\le t\le T}\int_{\mathbb R^6} f_t(x,v)e^{q\|v\|^2}\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Spatial integrability with Gaussian velocity weights holds uniformly on the regular interval, supplying the global integrability needed for collision noise.
- **claim-backward-propagator** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-target-propagator)): For $0\le a<b<2\beta$ and $0\le r\le t\le T$, there is a bounded backward propagator $U(r,t):E_a^0\to E_b^0$. It solves $$\label{eq:target-backward-mild} U(r,t)h=\mathsf S_{t-r}h +\int_r^t\mathsf S_{s-r}K_sU(s,t)h\,\mathrm ds,$$ has the composition law with intermediate exponents, and is the unique mild propagator in these scales. For $\phi\in C_c^\infty$, $$\label{eq:target-upper-end-identity} U(r,t)\phi-\phi=\int_r^t U(r,s)A_s\phi\,\mathrm ds.$$
  - Explanation: A unique backward mild evolution acts between test spaces with an exponent allowance and obeys composition and the upper-end identity.
- **claim-gaussian-target** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-gaussian-target)): The fluctuation equation in [thm:main](#thm:main) has a centered Gaussian solution on the test scales $E_a^0$, $0\le a<\beta$. It is a continuous linear map from each such test space to $L^2$ of its probability space, uniformly on $[0,T]$. Its covariance is $$\label{eq:target-covariance} \begin{split} \mathbb E[\zeta_t(\phi)\zeta_s(\psi)] ={}&\int f_0(z)\,U(0,t)\phi(z)\,U(0,s)\psi(z)\,\mathrm dz\\ &+\int_0^{\min(s,t)} C_r\bigl(U(r,t)\phi,U(r,s)\psi\bigr)\,\mathrm dr. \end{split}$$ It is unique in the class of solutions extending to these scales with uniformly bounded second-moment operator norms. In particular, the law required in [thm:main](#thm:main) is well defined.
  - Explanation: The backward propagator and independent Gaussian maps define the target covariance and give uniqueness within the stated second-moment class.
- **claim-kinetic-envelopes** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-kinetic-envelopes)): Under [ass:kinetic](#ass:kinetic), $$\label{eq:kinetic-bol-propagated} \sup_{0\le t\le T} \bigl(\\|f_t\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta} +\\|\nabla_x f_t\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta}\bigr)<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: The kinetic density and its spatial gradient retain spatially summable Gaussian envelopes throughout the interval.
- **claim-layered-analytic-package** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-dhm-package)): Assume [ass:kinetic](#ass:kinetic), the parameter hierarchy <a href="#eq:dhm-cutoffs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dhm-cutoffs">[eq:dhm-cutoffs]</a>–<a href="#eq:dhm-exponents" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dhm-exponents">[eq:dhm-exponents]</a>, and a common bound on the Gaussian Bol norms of all birth inputs. For sufficiently large fixed $L$ and sufficiently small $\epsilon$, the following hold.* 1. *For admissible dependent histories with prescribed labeled roots, fixed $b$, and complexity $\rho$, the sum of their normalized positive integrals is at most $$\label{eq:dhm-coarse} C^b\tau^{b/10}\epsilon^{c_*\rho} \|\log\epsilon\|^{C_*\rho}.$$ The sum includes the graph enumeration and the admissible indicator restrictions; a bounded observable multiplier costs its supremum. The birth inputs need not equal the approximate density constructed in the source, but must satisfy its common Gaussian Bol envelope.* 2. *Suppose the independent input $g$ in slab $i$ satisfies $$\label{eq:dhm-input-closeness} \\|g-f_{(i-1)\tau}\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta_{i-1}} \le\epsilon^{\theta_{i-1}}.$$ Let $\mathsf T_i^\epsilon[g]$ be the leading signed sum just defined. Then $$\label{eq:dhm-signed-trees} \\|\mathsf T_i^\epsilon[g]-f_{i\tau}\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta_i} \le\tfrac12\epsilon^{\theta_i}.$$ In the fixed-top-endpoint norm $\mathrm{Bol},\widetilde\beta_i$, the summed absolute contributions of cyclic members of $\mathcal T_i$, members of $\mathcal T_i^{\mathrm{err}}$, and tree members of $\mathcal T_i$ with at least $\Lambda_i/3$ lines are $o(\epsilon^{\theta_i})$. Fixed powers of the cutoffs, arising from a bounded number of selected edge positions, can be included. A fixed exponential factor per atom can also be included by choosing $L$ sufficiently large.* *Part (b) also holds uniformly when the final slab stops at any $0\le\sigma\le\tau$. With the same cutoffs and exponent budgets, write $\mathsf T_{i,\sigma}^\epsilon$ for its signed tree sum. Then $$\label{eq:dhm-partial-slab} \sup_{0\le\sigma\le\tau} \\|\mathsf T_{i,\sigma}^\epsilon[g] -f_{(i-1)\tau+\sigma}\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta_i} \le\tfrac12\epsilon^{\theta_i},$$ and the positive error classes obey the same full-$\tau$ upper majorants uniformly in $\sigma$. At $\sigma=0$ the map is the identity and its contact error classes vanish. The assertion in (b) is about the specified complete signed tree sum and positive error classes. Identifying a different one-root operator with that sum up to those errors requires a separate combinatorial argument.
  - Explanation: The imported positive history estimates control complexity, while the complete signed one-root tree sum approximates the Boltzmann density, including partial slabs.
- **claim-coarse-boundary-modifications** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-dhm-dummies)): The following modifications of the positive coarse bound are allowed.* 1. *If continuation condition (iv) is omitted in the top slab with $h$ roots, the bound may lose $\epsilon^{-C_*h}$.* 2. *A connected pure-C terminal component with at most $3\Lambda_i$ lines and excess at most $2\Gamma$, meeting a continuation below but having no designated top root, can be treated by adding one artificial root at cost $\mu$.* 3. *A fixed number $q$ of specified lines, continued to zero, can be supplied with initial-link continuations by adding $q$ dummy straight root lines at cost at most $\epsilon^{-q\upsilon}$, provided the root cutoffs have the corresponding fixed slack.* *Bounded records carried by these lines can be kept in the integral.
  - Explanation: Missing continuation, an unobserved terminal component, and selected lines requiring initial continuation can be handled with explicit normalization losses.
- **claim-autonomous-partition** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-autonomous-partition)): For a finite set $V$, $$\label{eq:autonomous-partition} \sum_{\mathcal C\in\mathcal P(V)} \prod_{C\in\mathcal C}\kappa_C \prod_{\{C,D\}\subseteq\mathcal C}\chi(C,D)=1.$$ Here $\mathcal P(V)$ denotes the partitions of $V$. If $S\subseteq V$ is a root set and $H$ tests the root histories, then $$\label{eq:autonomous-observation} H(\Psi_V\|_S)= \sum_{\mathcal C\in\mathcal P(V)} \prod_{C\in\mathcal C}\kappa_C \prod_{\{C,D\}\subseteq\mathcal C}\chi(C,D) H(\Psi_{\mathcal C}\|_S),$$ where $\Psi_{\mathcal C}$ is the juxtaposition of the autonomous histories.
  - Explanation: Exactly the collision-component partition contributes to the compatibility identity, and its autonomous histories reproduce the full truncated root observations.
- **claim-rooted-forest-stopping** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rooted-forest)): Let $W$ be a finite vertex set, let $R\subseteq W$ be nonempty, and attach $a_e\in\{0,1\}$ to each unordered pair of vertices. Fix $M\ge2$ and set $$\label{eq:ursell-block} u(B)=\sum_{\substack{G\text{ connected}\\V(G)=B}} \prod_{e\in E(G)}(-a_e),\qquad u(\{w\})=1.$$ There is a finite signed remainder $\mathcal R$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \label{eq:finite-rooted-identity} \prod_{e\subseteq W}(1-a_e) &=\sum_{I:\,R\subseteq I\subseteq W} \sum_{\substack{\mathcal B\in\mathcal P(I)\\ B\cap R\ne\varnothing,\ \|B\|<M\ (B\in\mathcal B)}} \prod_{B\in\mathcal B}u(B) \prod_{e\subseteq W\setminus I}(1-a_e)+\mathcal R. \end{aligned}$$ Its absolute value is bounded by a sum of rooted forests. Each forest in this bound has exactly one component with $M\le \|B\|\le2M-2$; its other components have fewer than $M$ vertices. All these components meet $R$. Vertices outside the forest retain their full mutual compatibility product. The coefficient of each selected forest in this domination is at most one. Moreover, $$\label{eq:ursell-tree-bound} \|u(B)\|\le\sum_{T\text{ spanning tree on }B}\prod_{e\in E(T)}a_e.$$
  - Explanation: Deterministic forest exploration produces the retained rooted blocks and a stopped remainder dominated by forests with a controlled oversized component. Connected block coefficients obey a tree bound.
- **claim-one-slab-variation-identity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-one-slab-expansion)): For a positive input point-process law, let $F'$ be its factorial hierarchy after the truncated slab. If its factorial measures up to the order in <a href="#eq:stopped-input-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stopped-input-order">[eq:stopped-input-order]</a> are finite, then $$\label{eq:one-slab-exact} F'_s=\mathcal L_sF+R_s,\qquad \|R_s\|\le\mathcal E_sF.$$ The same statements hold with prescribed records on root labels. The positive expression $\mathcal E_sF$ is a domination of $R_s$, not an assertion that the signed remainder has this local kernel.
  - Explanation: The actual slab hierarchy equals the retained operator plus a signed remainder dominated by a positive stopped operator; root records are preserved.
- **claim-one-root-bridge** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-one-root-bridge)): The difference between $\mathcal U_i(g)$ and the signed unrestricted one-root particle-tree sum with $n<\Lambda_i/3$ is dominated, after replacing inputs by $\|g\|$, by the following positive history integrals:* 1. *retained one-root molecules with $r\ge1$;* 2. *retained one-root molecules with $n\ge\Lambda_i/3$;* 3. *the small unrestricted trees with one additional incoming O contact inserted between a pair of their flight segments.* *The first two families are subsets of the DHM one-root graph class. The third is its additional-overlap error class. Coefficients in these dominations have at most a fixed power of the cutoffs. The leading tree coefficient is exactly $(-1)^{\#\mathrm O}$, with the labelled factorial just stated.
  - Explanation: The retained one-root map differs from the small unrestricted signed trees only through cycles, large molecules, and extra incoming encounters. The leading labelled coefficient is preserved.
- **claim-independent-one-root-factors** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-one-root)): The measures $g_i$ have Gaussian Bol-bounded density representatives. With the exponents $\beta_i,\theta_i$ of [thm:dhm-package](#thm:dhm-package), for sufficiently small $\epsilon$, $$\label{eq:one-root-convergence} \\|g_i-f_{i\tau}\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta_i}\le\epsilon^{\theta_i},\qquad \sup_{0\le i\le L}\\|g_i\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta_i}\le C.$$ The same estimates hold for the one-root map run through any portion $\sigma\in[0,\tau]$ of slab $i$, with target $f_{(i-1)\tau+\sigma}$ and exponent $\beta_i$.
  - Explanation: The recursively retained independent factors have density representatives with uniform Gaussian Bol bounds and approximate the kinetic density at full and partial slab endpoints.
- **claim-marked-regrouping** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-regrouping)): The retained hierarchy admits an exact finite expansion $$\label{eq:dependent-subsets} \widehat F^R_{i,S} =\sum_{H:\,R\subseteq H\subseteq S} E^R_{i,H}\otimes g_i^{\otimes(S\setminus H)}.$$ The coefficients can be represented by layered diagrams with the following rule. Every retained block in slab $i$ contains a root from the interface above it. A block is retained in $E^R_{i,H}$ if it contains at least two such roots, or it meets a dependent input label from the interface below. All other blocks sum to the independent factor $g_i$ on their unique root. Every record-bearing line continues to time zero. Forgetting any record commutes with <a href="#eq:retained-hierarchy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:retained-hierarchy">[eq:retained-hierarchy]</a>; at the diagram level it is effected by resumming the newly unmarked one-root histories.
  - Explanation: The finite retained hierarchy separates dependent root subsets from independent factors while continuing required records to the initial time. Forgetting records commutes with the hierarchy.
- **claim-component-factorials** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-diagram-labels)): The retained expansion factors over its global components. If $D^R_{i,B}$ is the sum of its diagrams connected on the designated roots $B$, then $$\label{eq:component-exponential-formula} \widehat F^R_{i,S} =\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal P(S)} \bigotimes_{B\in\pi}D^{R\cap B}_{i,B}.$$ Consequently factorial-cumulant inversion of the retained hierarchy keeps exactly its connected diagrams. This assertion is unchanged by the resummations into $g_i$.* *At fixed root order $h=\|S\|$ and bounded DHM complexity $\rho\le K$, let $n_i$ denote the number of particle lines in slab $i$, and $j_i$ its fresh input labels. The labelled denominator is $\prod_i j_i!$, where $$\label{eq:bounded-crossing-factorials} n_i-j_i=O_{K,h,m,L}(1),\qquad \prod_i\frac1{j_i!} \le\frac{(1+\sum_i n_i)^{C_{K,h,m,L}}}{\prod_i n_i!}.$$ Apart from the choices of ordinary contact pairs and their order, all distinguished-label, gluing, initial-block, and record-placement multiplicities are bounded by a fixed polynomial in total size times $C^b$, where $b$ is the atom count and the exponential base $C$ does not depend on $K$ or the moment order. In the coarse regime the same choices cost a power of the cutoffs with exponent $O(\rho)$, in addition to the exponential atom factor.
  - Explanation: Global components factor exactly, so factorial inversion selects connected diagrams. Bounded crossing counts preserve the fresh-label factorials up to polynomial factors.
- **claim-stopped-variation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stopped-variation)): Choose $L$ sufficiently large in terms of the fixed data and $\Gamma$. For any fixed bound on the number of recorded labels, there are $c_i>0$ such that $$\label{eq:retained-variation-error} \\|F^R_{i,S}-\widehat F^R_{i,S}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le e^{-c_i\Lambda_i}, \qquad \|S\|\le A_i/2.$$ The constants may decrease with $i$ and the fixed record bound. The statement is uniform for a partial final slab. No velocity weight is claimed for this error.
  - Explanation: Stopped expansion errors are exponentially small in total variation, uniformly over the permitted output orders and a partial final slab. No velocity weight is included.
- **claim-marked-component-expansion** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-marked-expansion)): Choose $\Gamma$ and then $L$ as above. These choices are independent of the moment order and the fixed observation list. On this single pasted dynamics the following statements hold.* 1. *The factorial hierarchy with any fixed finite list of records has the finite componentwise expansion <a href="#eq:retained-hierarchy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:retained-hierarchy">[eq:retained-hierarchy]</a>, with the variation error <a href="#eq:retained-variation-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:retained-variation-error">[eq:retained-variation-error]</a>. Its independent inputs are the $g_i$ in [prop:one-root](#prop:one-root); its label weights, continuations, and record projections are those of [lem:marked-regrouping](#lem:marked-regrouping), [lem:diagram-labels](#lem:diagram-labels).* 2. *At each fixed final order $h$, the actual normalized marked factorial cumulant $c_h^\epsilon$ is the sum of retained globally connected diagrams, plus an error of total variation at most $C_h e^{-c\Lambda_L}$. A nontrivial initial cumulant block is part of this connectivity. Dummy links are not.* 3. *For any fixed accuracy power $P$, one can choose a constant $K$ after the observation list and $h$ such that all remaining retained diagrams with $\rho>K$ have summed absolute variation at most $\epsilon^P$. At $\rho\le K$ the original fresh-label factorials satisfy <a href="#eq:bounded-crossing-factorials" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bounded-crossing-factorials">[eq:bounded-crossing-factorials]</a>. Neither increasing $K$ nor increasing a fixed moment order changes the exponential base per ordinary atom in the preceding estimates.
  - Explanation: A single pasted dynamics admits a finite marked component expansion whose connected diagrams give actual factorial cumulants up to negligible variation errors. High complexity can be removed after fixing the requested accuracy.
- **claim-terminal-witness-expansion** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-terminal-witness)): In slab $i$, let a terminal witness be a connected true C history on $n\le3\Lambda_i$ incoming labels, of excess at most $2\Gamma$, stopped at a contact time in that slab. It may carry arbitrary further nonnegative realizability restrictions. Sum its indicator with the full activity normalization $$\label{eq:terminal-witness-functional} \sum_n\frac{\mu^n}{n!} \int F_{i-1,n}(\mathrm dZ_n)\, \mathbf 1_{\text{terminal witness}}(Z_n).$$ For the terminal classes in (ii), this expression has the preceding retained layer expansion, with independent $g$ inputs and initial blocks. All lower C/O components attach through particle lines to the terminal witness. The preceding variation error is smaller than every fixed power of $\epsilon$. After the artificial-top-root normalization cost $\mu$, the coarse part with $\rho>K$ can also be made smaller than any assigned power by choosing $K$ sufficiently large. The remaining low-complexity witness integrals retain their realizability restrictions.
  - Explanation: An unobserved terminal collision witness admits a positive retained lower-layer expansion with the correct activity normalization and preserved realizability restrictions.
- **claim-sharp-connected-cumulants** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-sharp-cumulants)): Choose the parameters $\Gamma,L$ as in [thm:marked-expansion](#thm:marked-expansion), increasing $L$, if necessary, by an amount depending only on the uniform bounds for the Boltzmann solution and on $T$. This one choice works for every fixed factorial order and every fixed bounded observation list. For each fixed $h$, $$\label{eq:sharp-full-cumulant} \\|c_h^\epsilon\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le C_h\mu^{1-h}.$$ For $h=1,2$, the measures $\mu^{h-1}c_h^\epsilon$ converge against bounded continuous record tests to the signed sums of the retained global trees with trivial initial cumulants. At a contact their weight is the incoming flux $[(v-v_*)\cdot\omega]_+\,\mathrm dt\,\mathrm d\omega$; a scattering contact has sign $+1$, and a non-scattering contact has sign $-1$. The independent birth factors are $f_{i\tau}$.* *The sums of the absolute retained diagram measures, after multiplication by $\mu^{h-1}$, are bounded uniformly with a sufficiently small Gaussian weight in their designated endpoint velocities. The same bound holds for the limiting tree measures, and for the Gaussian weights in finitely many retained record velocities after decreasing the exponent. Among the retained connected diagrams, the sum of the absolute measures having a nontrivial initial cumulant or a global cycle is $o(\mu^{1-h})$. The assertions about Gaussian weights concern retained diagrams and their limits; no weighted estimate for a microscopic expansion remainder is asserted.
  - Explanation: Connected cumulants have the fluctuation-scale variation bound. The mean and connected pair limits are signed tree sums; cycles and nontrivial initial blocks vanish after scaling. Gaussian weights apply to retained diagrams and their limits.
- **claim-chronological-merger-coordinates** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sharp-merger-coordinates)): Fix a finite line graph, the C/O choices, the chronological order, and which contacts are redundant in that order. Away from nontransversal contacts and simultaneous events, its history integral has coordinates consisting of the birth velocities, one absolute translation for each global component, a vector in a ball of radius $R_{K,D}\epsilon$ for each physical initial merger, and $(t,\omega)\in\mathbb R\times S^2$ for each dynamic merger. The positional Jacobian at a dynamic merger is $$\label{eq:sharp-jacobian} \epsilon^2[(v-v_*)\cdot\omega]_+\,\mathrm dt\,\mathrm d\omega.$$ A specified redundant contact has no free continuous parameter and requires no extra Jacobian. The coordinate map has multiplicity at most one on each prescribed incoming straight-segment branch.
  - Explanation: A merger consumes a relative translation with its incoming-flux Jacobian. A redundant contact has already determined relative motion and is retained as a constraint without another continuous parameter.
- **claim-sharp-fresh-label-factorials** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sharp-factorials)): For diagrams of complexity at most $K$, the absolute sum with slab sizes $(n_1,\ldots,n_L)$ is bounded by a sum over chronologically ordered merger pair lists with denominator $\prod_i n_i!$, multiplied by $$\label{eq:sharp-polynomial-count} C_K(1+n)^{d_K}C^{\sum_i a_i}, \qquad n=\sum_i n_i,$$ where the base $C$ does not depend on $K$. The merger pairs themselves are not counted in <a href="#eq:sharp-polynomial-count" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sharp-polynomial-count">[eq:sharp-polynomial-count]</a>.
  - Explanation: The absolute bounded-complexity diagram sum retains slab factorial denominators, with polynomial exceptional-label costs and an exponential base independent of complexity.
- **claim-uniform-diagram-summability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sharp-summability)): For fixed $K$, the sum of the absolute connected diagram measures with slab sizes $(n_i)$, after removal of the factor $\mu^{1-h}$, is bounded by $$\label{eq:sharp-summable} C_K(1+n)^{d_K}(C\tau\sqrt L)^n.$$ The same bound, with the appropriate factor $\mu^{c-h}$, holds after a bounded number of initial links are erased. It also holds after prescribing the absolute spatial position of one observation in each component, or after inserting a sufficiently small Gaussian weight in the designated velocities.
  - Explanation: Joint energy and chronological pair estimates give a summable majorant. The bound also permits erased initial links, designated spatial traces, and small Gaussian endpoint weights.
- **claim-tree-extra-contact-nullsets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sharp-no-extra)): Consider a finite chronological forest with no physical initial links, with a prescribed C/O choice on each edge, on its incoming merger branches. Outside a null set of its merger parameters the following hold.* 1. *All specified contacts occur at distinct times away from interfaces and observation times and are transversal.* 2. *Before the first redundant contact, the demand for such a contact is false for all sufficiently small $\epsilon$.* 3. *On a full tree, an additional contact not in the prescribed list, including a contact preceding the prescribed joining of its two groups, is absent for all sufficiently small $\epsilon$.* *In (ii), the independent parameters used are those of the forest prefix; arbitrary remaining merger parameters may be appended. In (iii), an outgoing crossing following an O contact is not an additional contact, because only incoming crossings are counted.
  - Explanation: For a fixed chronological forest, generic merger parameters exclude unintended incoming contacts at sufficiently small diameter. The first redundant-contact indicator therefore vanishes almost everywhere.
- **claim-tree-spatial-traces** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sharp-spatial-traces)): Let $\mathcal T$ be any of the absolutely summable tree measures just constructed, and designate a line and a time at which its position is required. It has a version of the disintegration $$\label{eq:sharp-disintegration} \mathcal T=\mathrm dx\,\mathcal T_x$$ with respect to that position. For a sufficiently small $\alpha>0$, the Gaussian-weighted variation of $\mathcal T_x$ is bounded uniformly in $x$; its integral over $x$ is finite. For every bounded continuous test of the other coordinates, and also for continuous tests of smaller Gaussian growth, $x\mapsto\mathcal T_x(\Phi)$ is continuous.* *If two separate tree components are joined at their designated positions, the product of their conditional measures at a common position, integrated against incoming flux, is an absolutely defined measure. It is the result of imposing the joining position in the merger coordinates. Equivalently it is obtained by a smooth approximate identity for equality of the two positions. These facts apply to the marked one-root and connected two-root tree measures and their record projections.
  - Explanation: Absolutely summable tree measures have continuous spatial conditional versions with Gaussian variation bounds. Separate components can be joined by an absolutely defined conditional product at a common position.
- **claim-retained-tree-completeness** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sharp-tree-completeness)): For fixed designated roots, fixed required records, and fixed $L$, all global trees with trivial initial cumulants in the retained expansion after the one-root regrouping of [lem:marked-regrouping](#lem:marked-regrouping) have complexity at most a fixed constant $K_0$. This bound is independent of their number of ordinary contact vertices. Undoing and reapplying an unmarked one-root regrouping preserves their weights and their record projections.
  - Explanation: Required roots and records bound the complexity of every leading global tree independently of ordinary size. Unmarked singleton regrouping preserves weights and projections.
- **claim-last-contact-tree-products** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sharp-tree-splitting)): In the leading tree sums, deleting a last contact of the highest slab that meets a designated root splits its global tree into the product of the two component tree measures, with the incident lines designated before that contact. Their product at the collision position is the conditional product in [lem:sharp-spatial-traces](#lem:sharp-spatial-traces). Conversely, joining such two disjoint component histories at that contact gives exactly the corresponding retained tree, with its original factorial weight. Record portions not used by a test are projected out. These operations are valid for bounded continuous record tests and, with a smaller exponent, for tests of Gaussian velocity growth.
  - Explanation: Deleting a last relevant tree contact gives separate component measures whose spatial conditional product reconstructs the original tree with its factorial weight.
- **claim-marked-tree-evolution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-tree-evolution)): Between observation updates the limiting measures satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \partial_tF_t(\Phi) &=F_t(D\Phi)+\frac12\int BF_tF_{t,*}\Delta\Phi, \label{eq:limit-F-evolution}\\ \partial_tJ_t(\Phi\otimes\Psi) &=J_t\bigl((D+H_t)\Phi\otimes\Psi +\Phi\otimes(D+H_t)\Psi\bigr)\notag\\ &\quad+\int BF_tF_{t,*} \bigl(\Phi(\xi')\Psi(\xi_*') -\Phi(\xi)\Psi(\xi_*)\bigr). \label{eq:limit-J-evolution} \end{aligned}$$ The identities hold in transport mild form, and hence in weak differential form for smooth tests for which the displayed integrals exist. At an update $t_j$, $$\label{eq:limit-pushforwards} F_{t_j+}=(\Theta_j)_\#F_{t_j-}, \qquad J_{t_j+}=(\Theta_j\otimes\Theta_j)_\#J_{t_j-}.$$
  - Explanation: Last-contact splitting yields transport mild evolution for the marked mean and connected pair law, with deterministic pushforwards at observation updates.
- **claim-pasted-gaussian-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-cumulant-clt)): For the single pasted dynamics fixed above and every fixed observation list, $$\frac{\sum_i y_i(T)-\mathbb E\sum_i y_i(T)}{\sqrt\mu} \ \Longrightarrow\ \sum_j\zeta_{t_j}(\phi_j).$$ All centered moments of the scalar random variable on the left converge to the corresponding Gaussian moments.
  - Explanation: The exact-centered terminal mark sum on the fixed pasted dynamics converges to the corresponding Gaussian observation sum, together with all centered scalar moments.
- **claim-pasted-dynamics-accuracy** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-cutoff)): There is a sufficiently large dimensional integer $\Gamma_0$ with the following property. Fix $\Gamma\geq\Gamma_0$. There is $L_0=L_0(\Gamma,f_0,f,T,\beta)$ such that, for any fixed $L\geq L_0$, the true dynamics and the pasted dynamics of [thm:marked-expansion](#thm:marked-expansion), coupled using the same initial configuration, satisfy $$\label{eq:cutoff-probability} \mathbb P\{\text{the two trajectories differ at some time in }[0,T]\} \leq C\epsilon^{1+\eta}$$ for all sufficiently small $\epsilon$, where one may take $\eta=1/20$. Here the size cutoffs are the $A_i,\Lambda_i$ of [thm:dhm-package](#thm:dhm-package), and $C$ and the upper bound on $\epsilon$ may depend on the displayed fixed parameters.
  - Explanation: Coupled true and pasted trajectories differ with probability smaller than the fluctuation scale, after choosing the dimensional excess threshold and sufficiently many fixed slabs.
- **claim-high-birth-energy-removal** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff-energy-tail)): For the positive graph majorants in <a href="#eq:cutoff-low-complexity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cutoff-low-complexity">[eq:cutoff-low-complexity]</a>, there is a fixed power $H_*$ such that restricting the total birth energy $P=\sum_{j\text{ a birth}}\|v_j\|^2$ to $$P\leq P_{\epsilon}:=(1+\|\log\epsilon\|)^{H_*}$$ changes their sum by $O(\epsilon^M)$ for every fixed $M$.
  - Explanation: A fixed polylogarithmic birth-energy restriction removes a contribution smaller than every fixed requested power, before using local recollision gains.
- **claim-conditional-elementary-kernels** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-dhm-local)): Consider the positive kernel <a href="#eq:edge-integral" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:edge-integral">[eq:edge-integral]</a> of a regular piece, with its fixed ends prescribed. Suppose the support has $\|v_e\|\le \|\log\epsilon\|^{C_*}$, and each $x_e$ lies in a prescribed ball of radius $\|\log\epsilon\|^{C_*}$. The centers of those balls and all exterior variables may be arbitrary. Then:* 1. *A $\{2\}$ piece has kernel mass at most one. For a $\{3\}$ piece the kernel is parameterized by $$\label{eq:local-three-parameters} \mathrm dt\,\mathrm d\omega\,\mathrm dw\, \|(v-w)\cdot\omega\|,$$ with $(t,\omega,w)\in\mathbb R\times S^2\times\mathbb R^3$ and the appropriate incoming hemisphere. Here $v$ is the fixed-end velocity. If that end is outgoing, use the inverse elastic parametrization. All other states are determined by these parameters and the fixed end. A $\{4\}$ piece has in addition the measure $\mu\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv$. Consequently their masses are respectively bounded by $\|\log\epsilon\|^{C_*}$ and $\mu\|\log\epsilon\|^{C_*}$. A neutral type-A $\{33\}$ bound is obtained by a $\{3\}$ integration followed by a $\{2\}$ integration.* 2. *A $\{3\}$ piece gains $\epsilon^\upsilon$ over its neutral bound if its support satisfies any one of $$\label{eq:good-three-conditions} \begin{split} \|t-t_*\|&\le\epsilon^\upsilon,\qquad \|v_e-v_{e'}\|\le\epsilon^\upsilon,\\ \|x_e-x_*\|&\le\epsilon^{1-\upsilon},\qquad \|x_e-x_{e'}\|\le\epsilon^{1-\upsilon}. \end{split}$$ The two-edge conditions require distinct edges and, for O-atoms, exclude serial pairs. In the external-position condition $e$ cannot be the fixed edge, or, for an O-atom, its serial free edge. The quantities $t_*,x_*$ are fixed exterior parameters. The bound is uniform in their values.* 3. *For a type-B $\{33\}$ piece, both atoms are C. Suppose $\|t_1-t_2\|\ge\epsilon^\upsilon$, and at each of its atoms every pair of distinct incident edges satisfies $\|v_e-v_{e'}\|\ge\epsilon^\upsilon$. Its kernel mass is at most $\epsilon^\upsilon\|\log\epsilon\|^{C_*}$, uniformly in its two fixed ends.* *In all three assertions, multiplying by an arbitrary bounded function on the stated support costs its supremum. There is no assumption that a fixed-end velocity has an independent Gaussian distribution.
  - Explanation: Regular localized pieces have uniform conditional kernel bounds. Improved bounds retain all required closeness or nondegeneracy restrictions and do not assume an independent Gaussian law for fixed ends.
- **claim-pure-collision-cutting-alternatives** (construction, [source](#stmt-item-cutoff-main)): Let $M$ be a connected, full, realizable graph containing only scattering contacts. Full means that all its end variables are integrated. Define a transversal set $A\subset M$ to be a set for which $M\setminus A=A^-\sqcup A^+$, with no directed bond from $A^+$ to $A\cup A^-$, or from $A$ to $A^-$. Let $X_0(A)$ consist of vertices outside $A$ with two bonds to $A$, and let $X(A)$ be the closure obtained by repeatedly adding vertices with two bonds to the set already grown. The following statements hold; in parts <span class="upright">(ii)–(iv)</span>, $A$ is assumed to be connected.* 1. * There is a chain of connected transversal sets $A_1,\ldots,A_m=M$, with $A_1$ a tree, such that $X_0(A_j)\neq\varnothing$ for $j<m$, $$A_j\cup X(A_j)\subset A_{j+1},\qquad r(A_{j+1})=r(A_j\cup X(A_j)),\qquad r(A_{j+1})-r(A_j)\leq10\|X(A_j)\|.$$ The intermediate growth from $A_j\cup X(A_j)$ to $A_{j+1}$ adds one bond at each new vertex. For any $D$ transitions in this chain there is a cutting sequence of all of $M$ with one type $\{4\}$, no type $\{44\}$ or type-B pair, and at least $D$ type-A pairs.* 2. * One can choose $A^\pm$ such that each component of $A^+$ is attached to $A$. The two parts $X^\pm(A)=X(A)\cap A^\pm$ are equivalently obtained by adding vertices with two predecessors, or two successors, in the grown set. They are convex along particle lines. If $m_\pm$ is the number of bonds from $A$ to $X^\pm(A)$, then $$\|X^\pm(A)\|\leq G(m_\pm), \qquad G(q)=(2q+1)\binom q2(32q^{3/2})^{q^2}\quad(q\geq2).$$ The harmless values $G(0),G(1)$ may be replaced by one.* 3. * Put $z=\|X_0^\pm(A)\|$ and $a=r(A)$. There is a cutting sequence of $M$ having one type $\{4\}$, no type $\{44\}$ or type-B pair, and at least $z/2-a-1$ type-A pairs.* 4. *For this same choice of side there is a finite Borel partition of the contact-parameter domain, with a subcase-dependent integer $y\geq0$, and the following two available sequences in each subcase:* - *a sequence with one type $\{4\}$, no type $\{44\}$ or type-B pair, and at least $y/10-3(a+1)$ uniformly good singletons;* - *a sequence with no type $\{44\}$, at most $S=a+z+y$ type $\{4\}$ singletons, all type-B pairs uniformly good, and at least $$\frac{m_\pm-10^5S}{10}$$ type-A or type-B pairs in total.* *The singleton and pair types not counted for a gain have the neutral kernels described above. On graphs of polylogarithmic size and bounded excess the indicated partitions and operation sequences have at most $C^b\|\log\epsilon\|^{C_*}$ subcases. The exponential base $C$ depends on dimensional choices, not on $L$. The required local restrictions at each uniformly good piece involve its own variables and data exposed earlier in the cutting order.
  - Explanation: Imported transversal growth and realizability bounds provide cutting sequences with controlled free pieces and improved singleton or pair counts, preserving the conditional restrictions needed for their gains.
- **claim-neutral-cut-completion** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff-neutral)): Suppose an exposed set is order-convex in the directed contact graph. Every remaining component touching this set can be cut using only type $\{2\}$ and type $\{3\}$ singletons. The same conclusion holds with given top ends in place of the exposed set. Scattering and nonscattering contacts are both allowed.
  - Explanation: An order-convex exposed set or prescribed top ends allow all touching components to be completed with neutral singleton cuts, including nonscattering contacts.
- **claim-adjacent-pair-gains** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff-pair)): Consider every possible choice $e_1,e_2$ of the two prospective fixed edges of a type-A pair, with $t_1,t_2$ the corresponding contact times. Call that choice strongly close if $$\label{eq:cutoff-close} \|t_2-t_1\|\leq\epsilon^{9/10},\qquad \|v_{e_2}-v_{e_1}\|\leq\epsilon^{4/5}.$$ Then:* 1. *a primitive pair satisfying <a href="#eq:cutoff-close" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cutoff-close">[eq:cutoff-close]</a>, cut freely as one piece, has mass at most $$\label{eq:cutoff-free-pair} C R^C\mu\epsilon^{9/10+3(4/5)} =C R^C\epsilon^{13/10};$$* 2. *in the absence of every strongly close primitive pair, each type-A pair used by a cutting sequence has conditional mass at most $C R^C\epsilon^{1/20}$, or may be split into a neutral type-two piece and a singleton with this same improved bound.* *The conditional bounds are uniform in the previously exposed lines and times.
  - Explanation: A strongly close primitive scattering pair gains directly in time and velocity volume. Otherwise type-A pairs have a uniform conditional gain, including nonprimitive close pairs with an exposed intermediate time.
- **claim-amplified-collision-cut** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff-amplified)): For a sufficiently large dimensional $\Gamma_0$, every connected realizable full scattering graph with $\Gamma<r(M)\leq2\Gamma$, $\Gamma\geq\Gamma_0$, has a finite decomposition into cut sequences of the following form. Either it contains a freely cut pair estimated by <a href="#eq:cutoff-free-pair" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cutoff-free-pair">[eq:cutoff-free-pair]</a> and no other activity loss, or its retained improved pieces and free singletons satisfy $$\label{eq:cutoff-gain-balance} \delta\,g-2s_4\geq6/5 .$$ Here $g$ counts the retained improved pieces, and $s_4$ counts free singleton pieces. The number of subcases is at most $C^b\|\log\epsilon\|^{C_*}$. The number of pieces other than neutral type-three singletons is bounded in terms of $\Gamma$ and the fixed choices.
  - Explanation: A realizable terminal graph above the excess threshold either contains the improved primitive seed or supplies enough retained geometric gains to pay all free-singleton activity losses.
- **claim-literal-parameter-extraction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff-extraction)): Consider a finite ordered composition of positive cut kernels. At an ordinary step its free variables are $\vartheta_j=(t_j,\omega_j,w_j)$, with measure $$a_j\,\mathrm dt_j\,\mathrm d\omega_j\,\mathrm dw_j ,$$ followed by a deterministic update of the exposed data. Suppose every exceptional kernel $K_\ell(h,\mathrm dz)$ has mass at most $H_\ell$, uniformly in admissible data $h$ from earlier cuts. Include in that kernel every restriction needed for this bound, and set it to zero outside its admissibility domain.* *Remove the factors $a_j$ from the ordinary measures, obtaining a positive measure $\nu$. If $E$ is a set of complete histories and $\mathcal D$ is a measurable set containing its projection onto the literal variables $\vartheta=(\vartheta_1,\ldots,\vartheta_q)$, then $$\label{eq:cutoff-extraction} \nu(E)\leq \left(\prod_\ell H_\ell\right) \int_{\mathcal D}\prod_{j=1}^q \mathrm dt_j\,\mathrm d\omega_j\,\mathrm dw_j .$$ The same assertion holds when the ordinary density after division by $a_j$ is merely bounded by one.
  - Explanation: Positive conditional kernels allow ordinary literal parameters to be extracted while exceptional mass bounds remain conditional on earlier exposed data. A containing projection bounds the remaining parameter volume.
- **claim-common-velocity-containing-set** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff-ellipsoid)): Fix one original history graph and its ordinary normals. Suppose the sum of squared velocities at all its births is at most $P$, and suppose there are $s$ exceptional atoms, counting both atoms of a pair. For $q\geq1$, the concatenated ordinary parameters $W=(w_1,\ldots,w_q)$ lie in a set of volume at most $$\label{eq:cutoff-ellipsoid} C_s\{(1+P)(1+q)\}^{C s} \left(C\sqrt{1+P/q}\right)^{3q}.$$ The exponential base $C$ is absolute. The containing set depends on the ordinary normals and the graph but not on the numerical contact times or on the exceptional parameters. If $P,q$ are bounded by fixed powers of $\|\log\epsilon\|$, the prefactor is a fixed power of $\|\log\epsilon\|$.
  - Explanation: Chronological orthogonal velocity updates and a finite exceptional-parameter net produce a common volume bound for all ordinary velocities without independent velocity boxes.
- **claim-causal-time-speed-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff-time)): In a slab containing $q_i$ ordinary atoms, retain the partial order induced on those atoms by the full original graph, including chains through exceptional atoms. Let $\mathcal T_i$ be its time-order region in that slab. On the original support with total birth energy at most $P$, $$\label{eq:cutoff-time} \|\mathcal T_i\|\, \sup \prod_{a\text{ ordinary in slab }i}\|v_a-v_{*a}\| \leq \frac{\tau^{q_i}(2q_iP)^{q_i/2}}{q_i!}.$$ An empty slab contributes the factor one.
  - Explanation: Causal antichains have disjoint incoming pairs and share the birth-energy budget. Weighted enumeration of linear extensions bounds time volume times the relative-speed product.
- **claim-exceptional-piece-summation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff-sum)): Consider the positive low-complexity graph integrals obtained above. Suppose a cut sequence has $q=b-O_K(1)$ ordinary atoms and boundedly many exceptional pieces, whose conditional mass bounds have product $\epsilon^\alpha\|\log\epsilon\|^{C_*}$. After summing spatial cells and energy bins in a polylogarithmic energy region, its integral is at most $$\label{eq:cutoff-sum} \epsilon^\alpha\|\log\epsilon\|^{C_*} (C\tau\sqrt L)^q .$$ The exponential base $C$ depends on the fixed kinetic envelopes and dimension, but not on $K,L$.
  - Explanation: Spatial-cell sums, common velocity domains, causal time order, and dyadic energy integration preserve the exceptional gains while giving geometric contraction in ordinary atom count.
- **claim-exact-centering-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-center-transfer)): Let the true and pasted dynamics start from the same initial ensemble. Under the cutoff estimate of [prop:cutoff](#prop:cutoff), the expectation of any fixed bounded empirical observation list differs between the two dynamics by $o(\mu^{-1/2})$. Consequently the corresponding exact-centered fluctuation vectors have the same weak limits.
  - Explanation: The discrepancy probability and particle-number moments control expectation differences at the fluctuation scale, transferring exact-centered finite observation limits to the true dynamics.

          "exact_quote": "The function $f_0\\ge0$ is a smooth probability density and, for some $\\beta>0$, $$\\label{eq:initial-bol-bounds}\n \\|f_0\\|_{\\mathrm{Bol},2\\beta}\n +\\|\\nabla_xf_0\\|_{\\mathrm{Bol},2\\beta}<\\infty.$$ There is a nonnegative classical solution $f$ of <a href=\"#eq:boltzmann\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:boltzmann\">[eq:boltzmann]</a> with initial datum $f_0$ on a prescribed finite interval $[0,T]$, $T>0$, such that $$\\label{eq:kinetic-gaussian}\n M:=\\sup_{0\\le t\\le T}\\sup_{x,v}\n       e^{2\\beta|v|^2}f(t,x,v)<\\infty.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-target-propagator",
        "exact_quote": "For $0\\le a<b<2\\beta$ and $0\\le r\\le t\\le T$, there is a bounded backward propagator $U(r,t):E_a^0\\to E_b^0$. It solves $$\\label{eq:target-backward-mild}\n U(r,t)h=\\mathsf S_{t-r}h\n   +\\int_r^t\\mathsf S_{s-r}K_sU(s,t)h\\,\\mathrm ds,$$ has the composition law with intermediate exponents, and is the unique mild propagator in these scales. For $\\phi\\in C_c^\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:target-upper-end-identity}\n U(r,t)\\phi-\\phi=\\int_r^t U(r,s)A_s\\phi\\,\\mathrm ds.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-gaussian-target",
        "exact_quote": "The fluctuation equation in [thm:main](#thm:main) has a centered Gaussian solution on the test scales $E_a^0$, $0\\le a<\\beta$. It is a continuous linear map from each such test space to $L^2$ of its probability space, uniformly on $[0,T]$. Its covariance is $$\\label{eq:target-covariance}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathbb E[\\zeta_t(\\phi)\\zeta_s(\\psi)]\n ={}&\\int f_0(z)\\,U(0,t)\\phi(z)\\,U(0,s)\\psi(z)\\,\\mathrm dz\\\\\n &+\\int_0^{\\min(s,t)}\n     C_r\\bigl(U(r,t)\\phi,U(r,s)\\psi\\bigr)\\,\\mathrm dr.\n \\end{split}$$ It is unique in the class of solutions extending to these scales with uniformly bounded second-moment operator norms. In particular, the law required in [thm:main](#thm:main) is well defined.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-gaussian-target",

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-dhm-package",
        "exact_quote": "Assume [ass:kinetic](#ass:kinetic), the parameter hierarchy <a href=\"#eq:dhm-cutoffs\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dhm-cutoffs\">[eq:dhm-cutoffs]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:dhm-exponents\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dhm-exponents\">[eq:dhm-exponents]</a>, and a common bound on the Gaussian Bol norms of all birth inputs. For sufficiently large fixed $L$ and sufficiently small $\\epsilon$, the following hold.*\n\n\n1.  *For admissible dependent histories with prescribed labeled roots, fixed $b$, and complexity $\\rho$, the sum of their normalized positive integrals is at most $$\\label{eq:dhm-coarse}\n     C^b\\tau^{b/10}\\epsilon^{c_*\\rho}\n           |\\log\\epsilon|^{C_*\\rho}.$$ The sum includes the graph enumeration and the admissible indicator restrictions; a bounded observable multiplier costs its supremum. The birth inputs need not equal the approximate density constructed in the source, but must satisfy its common Gaussian Bol envelope.*\n\n\n\n2.  *Suppose the independent input $g$ in slab $i$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:dhm-input-closeness}\n     \\|g-f_{(i-1)\\tau}\\|_{\\mathrm{Bol},\\beta_{i-1}}\n           \\le\\epsilon^{\\theta_{i-1}}.$$ Let $\\mathsf T_i^\\epsilon[g]$ be the leading signed sum just defined. Then $$\\label{eq:dhm-signed-trees}\n     \\|\\mathsf T_i^\\epsilon[g]-f_{i\\tau}\\|_{\\mathrm{Bol},\\beta_i}\n           \\le\\tfrac12\\epsilon^{\\theta_i}.$$ In the fixed-top-endpoint norm $\\mathrm{Bol},\\widetilde\\beta_i$, the summed absolute contributions of cyclic members of $\\mathcal T_i$, members of $\\mathcal T_i^{\\mathrm{err}}$, and tree members of $\\mathcal T_i$ with at least $\\Lambda_i/3$ lines are $o(\\epsilon^{\\theta_i})$. Fixed powers of the cutoffs, arising from a bounded number of selected edge positions, can be included. A fixed exponential factor per atom can also be included by choosing $L$ sufficiently large.*\n\n\n*Part (b) also holds uniformly when the final slab stops at any $0\\le\\sigma\\le\\tau$. With the same cutoffs and exponent budgets, write $\\mathsf T_{i,\\sigma}^\\epsilon$ for its signed tree sum. Then $$\\label{eq:dhm-partial-slab}\n \\sup_{0\\le\\sigma\\le\\tau}\n \\|\\mathsf T_{i,\\sigma}^\\epsilon[g]\n          -f_{(i-1)\\tau+\\sigma}\\|_{\\mathrm{Bol},\\beta_i}\n       \\le\\tfrac12\\epsilon^{\\theta_i},$$ and the positive error classes obey the same full-$\\tau$ upper majorants uniformly in $\\sigma$. At $\\sigma=0$ the map is the identity and its contact error classes vanish. The assertion in (b) is about the specified complete signed tree sum and positive error classes. Identifying a different one-root operator with that sum up to those errors requires a separate combinatorial argument.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },

The birth inputs need not equal the approximate density constructed in the source, but must satisfy its common Gaussian Bol envelope.*\n\n\n\n2.  *Suppose the independent input $g$ in slab $i$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:dhm-input-closeness}\n     \\|g-f_{(i-1)\\tau}\\|_{\\mathrm{Bol},\\beta_{i-1}}\n           \\le\\epsilon^{\\theta_{i-1}}.$$ Let $\\mathsf T_i^\\epsilon[g]$ be the leading signed sum just defined.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-dhm-dummies",
        "exact_quote": "The following modifications of the positive coarse bound are allowed.*\n\n1.  *If continuation condition (iv) is omitted in the top slab with $h$ roots, the bound may lose $\\epsilon^{-C_*h}$.*\n\n2.  *A connected pure-C terminal component with at most $3\\Lambda_i$ lines and excess at most $2\\Gamma$, meeting a continuation below but having no designated top root, can be treated by adding one artificial root at cost $\\mu$.*\n\n3.  *A fixed number $q$ of specified lines, continued to zero, can be supplied with initial-link continuations by adding $q$ dummy straight root lines at cost at most $\\epsilon^{-q\\upsilon}$, provided the root cutoffs have the corresponding fixed slack.*\n\n*Bounded records carried by these lines can be kept in the integral.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-dhm-dummies",
          "exact_quote": "The following modifications of the positive coarse bound are allowed.*\n\n1.  *If continuation condition (iv) is omitted in the top slab with $h$ roots, the bound may lose $\\epsilon^{-C_*h}$.*\n\n2.  *A connected pure-C terminal component with at most $3\\Lambda_i$ lines and excess at most $2\\Gamma$, meeting a continuation below but having no designated top root, can be treated by adding one artificial root at cost $\\mu$.*\n\n3.  *A fixed number $q$ of specified lines, continued to zero, can be supplied with initial-link continuations by adding $q$ dummy straight root lines at cost at most $\\epsilon^{-q\\upsilon}$, provided the root cutoffs have the corresponding fixed slack.*\n\n*Bounded records carried by these lines can be kept in the integral.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-autonomous-partition",
        "exact_quote": "For a finite set $V$, $$\\label{eq:autonomous-partition}\n \\sum_{\\mathcal C\\in\\mathcal P(V)}\n \\prod_{C\\in\\mathcal C}\\kappa_C\n \\prod_{\\{C,D\\}\\subseteq\\mathcal C}\\chi(C,D)=1.$$ Here $\\mathcal P(V)$ denotes the partitions of $V$. If $S\\subseteq V$ is a root set and $H$ tests the root histories, then $$\\label{eq:autonomous-observation}\n H(\\Psi_V|_S)=\n \\sum_{\\mathcal C\\in\\mathcal P(V)}\n \\prod_{C\\in\\mathcal C}\\kappa_C\n \\prod_{\\{C,D\\}\\subseteq\\mathcal C}\\chi(C,D)\n H(\\Psi_{\\mathcal C}|_S),$$ where $\\Psi_{\\mathcal C}$ is the juxtaposition of the autonomous histories.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-autonomous-partition",
          "exact_quote": "For a finite set $V$, $$\\label{eq:autonomous-partition}\n \\sum_{\\mathcal C\\in\\mathcal P(V)}\n \\prod_{C\\in\\mathcal C}\\kappa_C\n \\prod_{\\{C,D\\}\\subseteq\\mathcal C}\\chi(C,D)=1.$$ Here $\\mathcal P(V)$ denotes the partitions of $V$. If $S\\subseteq V$ is a root set and $H$ tests the root histories, then $$\\label{eq:autonomous-observation}\n H(\\Psi_V|_S)=\n \\sum_{\\mathcal C\\in\\mathcal P(V)}\n \\prod_{C\\in\\mathcal C}\\kappa_C\n \\prod_{\\{C,D\\}\\subseteq\\mathcal C}\\chi(C,D)\n H(\\Psi_{\\mathcal C}|_S),$$ where $\\Psi_{\\mathcal C}$ is the juxtaposition of the autonomous histories.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rooted-forest",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a finite vertex set, let $R\\subseteq W$ be nonempty, and attach $a_e\\in\\{0,1\\}$ to each unordered pair of vertices. Fix $M\\ge2$ and set $$\\label{eq:ursell-block}\n u(B)=\\sum_{\\substack{G\\text{ connected}\\\\V(G)=B}}\n             \\prod_{e\\in E(G)}(-a_e),\\qquad u(\\{w\\})=1.$$ There is a finite signed remainder $\\mathcal R$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{eq:finite-rooted-identity}\n \\prod_{e\\subseteq W}(1-a_e)\n &=\\sum_{I:\\,R\\subseteq I\\subseteq W}\n   \\sum_{\\substack{\\mathcal B\\in\\mathcal P(I)\\\\\n               B\\cap R\\ne\\varnothing,\\ |B|<M\\ (B\\in\\mathcal B)}}\n       \\prod_{B\\in\\mathcal B}u(B)\n       \\prod_{e\\subseteq W\\setminus I}(1-a_e)+\\mathcal R.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Its absolute value is bounded by a sum of rooted forests. Each forest in this bound has exactly one component with $M\\le |B|\\le2M-2$; its other components have fewer than $M$ vertices. All these components meet $R$. Vertices outside the forest retain their full mutual compatibility product. The coefficient of each selected forest in this domination is at most one. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:ursell-tree-bound}\n |u(B)|\\le\\sum_{T\\text{ spanning tree on }B}\\prod_{e\\in E(T)}a_e.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-one-root-bridge",
        "exact_quote": "The difference between $\\mathcal U_i(g)$ and the signed unrestricted one-root particle-tree sum with $n<\\Lambda_i/3$ is dominated, after replacing inputs by $|g|$, by the following positive history integrals:*\n\n1.  *retained one-root molecules with $r\\ge1$;*\n\n2.  *retained one-root molecules with $n\\ge\\Lambda_i/3$;*\n\n3.  *the small unrestricted trees with one additional incoming O contact inserted between a pair of their flight segments.*\n\n*The first two families are subsets of the DHM one-root graph class. The third is its additional-overlap error class. Coefficients in these dominations have at most a fixed power of the cutoffs. The leading tree coefficient is exactly $(-1)^{\\#\\mathrm O}$, with the labelled factorial just stated.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-marked-regrouping",
        "exact_quote": "The retained hierarchy admits an exact finite expansion $$\\label{eq:dependent-subsets}\n \\widehat F^R_{i,S}\n   =\\sum_{H:\\,R\\subseteq H\\subseteq S}\n        E^R_{i,H}\\otimes g_i^{\\otimes(S\\setminus H)}.$$ The coefficients can be represented by layered diagrams with the following rule. Every retained block in slab $i$ contains a root from the interface above it. A block is retained in $E^R_{i,H}$ if it contains at least two such roots, or it meets a dependent input label from the interface below. All other blocks sum to the independent factor $g_i$ on their unique root. Every record-bearing line continues to time zero. Forgetting any record commutes with <a href=\"#eq:retained-hierarchy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:retained-hierarchy\">[eq:retained-hierarchy]</a>; at the diagram level it is effected by resumming the newly unmarked one-root histories.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-diagram-labels",
        "exact_quote": "The retained expansion factors over its global components. If $D^R_{i,B}$ is the sum of its diagrams connected on the designated roots $B$, then $$\\label{eq:component-exponential-formula}\n \\widehat F^R_{i,S}\n       =\\sum_{\\pi\\in\\mathcal P(S)}\n                         \\bigotimes_{B\\in\\pi}D^{R\\cap B}_{i,B}.$$ Consequently factorial-cumulant inversion of the retained hierarchy keeps exactly its connected diagrams. This assertion is unchanged by the resummations into $g_i$.*\n\n\n*At fixed root order $h=|S|$ and bounded DHM complexity $\\rho\\le K$, let $n_i$ denote the number of particle lines in slab $i$, and $j_i$ its fresh input labels. The labelled denominator is $\\prod_i j_i!$, where $$\\label{eq:bounded-crossing-factorials}\n n_i-j_i=O_{K,h,m,L}(1),\\qquad\n \\prod_i\\frac1{j_i!}\n      \\le\\frac{(1+\\sum_i n_i)^{C_{K,h,m,L}}}{\\prod_i n_i!}.$$ Apart from the choices of ordinary contact pairs and their order, all distinguished-label, gluing, initial-block, and record-placement multiplicities are bounded by a fixed polynomial in total size times $C^b$, where $b$ is the atom count and the exponential base $C$ does not depend on $K$ or the moment order. In the coarse regime the same choices cost a power of the cutoffs with exponent $O(\\rho)$, in addition to the exponential atom factor.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stopped-variation",
        "exact_quote": "Choose $L$ sufficiently large in terms of the fixed data and $\\Gamma$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-marked-expansion",
        "exact_quote": "Choose $\\Gamma$ and then $L$ as above. These choices are independent of the moment order and the fixed observation list. On this single pasted dynamics the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *The factorial hierarchy with any fixed finite list of records has the finite componentwise expansion <a href=\"#eq:retained-hierarchy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:retained-hierarchy\">[eq:retained-hierarchy]</a>, with the variation error <a href=\"#eq:retained-variation-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:retained-variation-error\">[eq:retained-variation-error]</a>. Its independent inputs are the $g_i$ in [prop:one-root](#prop:one-root); its label weights, continuations, and record projections are those of [lem:marked-regrouping](#lem:marked-regrouping), [lem:diagram-labels](#lem:diagram-labels).*\n\n2.  *At each fixed final order $h$, the actual normalized marked factorial cumulant $c_h^\\epsilon$ is the sum of retained globally connected diagrams, plus an error of total variation at most $C_h e^{-c\\Lambda_L}$. A nontrivial initial cumulant block is part of this connectivity. Dummy links are not.*\n\n3.  *For any fixed accuracy power $P$, one can choose a constant $K$ after the observation list and $h$ such that all remaining retained diagrams with $\\rho>K$ have summed absolute variation at most $\\epsilon^P$. At $\\rho\\le K$ the original fresh-label factorials satisfy <a href=\"#eq:bounded-crossing-factorials\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:bounded-crossing-factorials\">[eq:bounded-crossing-factorials]</a>. Neither increasing $K$ nor increasing a fixed moment order changes the exponential base per ordinary atom in the preceding estimates.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-marked-expansion",

Its independent inputs are the $g_i$ in [prop:one-root](#prop:one-root); its label weights, continuations, and record projections are those of [lem:marked-regrouping](#lem:marked-regrouping), [lem:diagram-labels](#lem:diagram-labels).*\n\n2.  *At each fixed final order $h$, the actual normalized marked factorial cumulant $c_h^\\epsilon$ is the sum of retained globally connected diagrams, plus an error of total variation at most $C_h e^{-c\\Lambda_L}$.         "anchor": "stmt-cor-terminal-witness",
        "exact_quote": "In slab $i$, let a terminal witness be a connected true C history on $n\\le3\\Lambda_i$ incoming labels, of excess at most $2\\Gamma$, stopped at a contact time in that slab. It may carry arbitrary further nonnegative realizability restrictions. Sum its indicator with the full activity normalization $$\\label{eq:terminal-witness-functional}\n \\sum_n\\frac{\\mu^n}{n!}\n       \\int F_{i-1,n}(\\mathrm dZ_n)\\,\n                       \\mathbf 1_{\\text{terminal witness}}(Z_n).$$ For the terminal classes in (ii), this expression has the preceding retained layer expansion, with independent $g$ inputs and initial blocks. All lower C/O components attach through particle lines to the terminal witness. The preceding variation error is smaller than every fixed power of $\\epsilon$. After the artificial-top-root normalization cost $\\mu$, the coarse part with $\\rho>K$ can also be made smaller than any assigned power by choosing $K$ sufficiently large. The remaining low-complexity witness integrals retain their realizability restrictions.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-sharp-cumulants",
        "exact_quote": "Choose the parameters $\\Gamma,L$ as in [thm:marked-expansion](#thm:marked-expansion), increasing $L$, if necessary, by an amount depending only on the uniform bounds for the Boltzmann solution and on $T$. This one choice works for every fixed factorial order and every fixed bounded observation list. For each fixed $h$, $$\\label{eq:sharp-full-cumulant}\n \\|c_h^\\epsilon\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\le C_h\\mu^{1-h}.$$ For $h=1,2$, the measures $\\mu^{h-1}c_h^\\epsilon$ converge against bounded continuous record tests to the signed sums of the retained global trees with trivial initial cumulants. At a contact their weight is the incoming flux $[(v-v_*)\\cdot\\omega]_+\\,\\mathrm dt\\,\\mathrm d\\omega$; a scattering contact has sign $+1$, and a non-scattering contact has sign $-1$. The independent birth factors are $f_{i\\tau}$.*\n\n*The sums of the absolute retained diagram measures, after multiplication by $\\mu^{h-1}$, are bounded uniformly with a sufficiently small Gaussian weight in their designated endpoint velocities. The same bound holds for the limiting tree measures, and for the Gaussian weights in finitely many retained record velocities after decreasing the exponent. Among the retained connected diagrams, the sum of the absolute measures having a nontrivial initial cumulant or a global cycle is $o(\\mu^{1-h})$. The assertions about Gaussian weights concern retained diagrams and their limits; no weighted estimate for a microscopic expansion remainder is asserted.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

At a contact their weight is the incoming flux $[(v-v_*)\\cdot\\omega]_+\\,\\mathrm dt\\,\\mathrm d\\omega$; a scattering contact has sign $+1$, and a non-scattering contact has sign $-1$. The independent birth factors are $f_{i\\tau}$.*\n\n*The sums of the absolute retained diagram measures, after multiplication by $\\mu^{h-1}$, are bounded uniformly with a sufficiently small Gaussian weight in their designated endpoint velocities. The same bound holds for the limiting tree measures, and for the Gaussian weights in finitely many retained record velocities after decreasing the exponent. Among the retained connected diagrams, the sum of the absolute measures having a nontrivial initial cumulant or a global cycle is $o(\\mu^{1-h})$. The assertions about Gaussian weights concern retained diagrams and their limits; no weighted estimate for a microscopic expansion remainder is asserted.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-merger-coordinates",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite line graph, the C/O choices, the chronological order, and which contacts are redundant in that order. Away from nontransversal contacts and simultaneous events, its history integral has coordinates consisting of the birth velocities, one absolute translation for each global component, a vector in a ball of radius $R_{K,D}\\epsilon$ for each physical initial merger, and $(t,\\omega)\\in\\mathbb R\\times S^2$ for each dynamic merger. The positional Jacobian at a dynamic merger is $$\\label{eq:sharp-jacobian}\n       \\epsilon^2[(v-v_*)\\cdot\\omega]_+\\,\\mathrm dt\\,\\mathrm d\\omega.$$ A specified redundant contact has no free continuous parameter and requires no extra Jacobian. The coordinate map has multiplicity at most one on each prescribed incoming straight-segment branch.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },

The coordinate map has multiplicity at most one on each prescribed incoming straight-segment branch.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-no-extra",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a finite chronological forest with no physical initial links, with a prescribed C/O choice on each edge, on its incoming merger branches. Outside a null set of its merger parameters the following hold.*\n\n1.  *All specified contacts occur at distinct times away from interfaces and observation times and are transversal.*\n\n2.  *Before the first redundant contact, the demand for such a contact is false for all sufficiently small $\\epsilon$.*\n\n3.  *On a full tree, an additional contact not in the prescribed list, including a contact preceding the prescribed joining of its two groups, is absent for all sufficiently small $\\epsilon$.*\n\n*In (ii), the independent parameters used are those of the forest prefix; arbitrary remaining merger parameters may be appended. In (iii), an outgoing crossing following an O contact is not an additional contact, because only incoming crossings are counted.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-spatial-traces",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal T$ be any of the absolutely summable tree measures just constructed, and designate a line and a time at which its position is required. It has a version of the disintegration $$\\label{eq:sharp-disintegration}\n               \\mathcal T=\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathcal T_x$$ with respect to that position. For a sufficiently small $\\alpha>0$, the Gaussian-weighted variation of $\\mathcal T_x$ is bounded uniformly in $x$; its integral over $x$ is finite. For every bounded continuous test of the other coordinates, and also for continuous tests of smaller Gaussian growth, $x\\mapsto\\mathcal T_x(\\Phi)$ is continuous.*\n\n*If two separate tree components are joined at their designated positions, the product of their conditional measures at a common position, integrated against incoming flux, is an absolutely defined measure. It is the result of imposing the joining position in the merger coordinates. Equivalently it is obtained by a smooth approximate identity for equality of the two positions. These facts apply to the marked one-root and connected two-root tree measures and their record projections.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-tree-completeness",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed designated roots, fixed required records, and fixed $L$, all global trees with trivial initial cumulants in the retained expansion after the one-root regrouping of [lem:marked-regrouping](#lem:marked-regrouping) have complexity at most a fixed constant $K_0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-tree-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "In the leading tree sums, deleting a last contact of the highest slab that meets a designated root splits its global tree into the product of the two component tree measures, with the incident lines designated before that contact. Their product at the collision position is the conditional product in [lem:sharp-spatial-traces](#lem:sharp-spatial-traces). Conversely, joining such two disjoint component histories at that contact gives exactly the corresponding retained tree, with its original factorial weight. Record portions not used by a test are projected out. These operations are valid for bounded continuous record tests and, with a smaller exponent, for tests of Gaussian velocity growth.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-tree-evolution",
        "exact_quote": "Between observation updates the limiting measures satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\partial_tF_t(\\Phi)\n  &=F_t(D\\Phi)+\\frac12\\int BF_tF_{t,*}\\Delta\\Phi,\n       \\label{eq:limit-F-evolution}\\\\\n \\partial_tJ_t(\\Phi\\otimes\\Psi)\n  &=J_t\\bigl((D+H_t)\\Phi\\otimes\\Psi\n                     +\\Phi\\otimes(D+H_t)\\Psi\\bigr)\\notag\\\\\n  &\\quad+\\int BF_tF_{t,*}\n          \\bigl(\\Phi(\\xi')\\Psi(\\xi_*')\n                         -\\Phi(\\xi)\\Psi(\\xi_*)\\bigr).\n       \\label{eq:limit-J-evolution}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The identities hold in transport mild form, and hence in weak differential form for smooth tests for which the displayed integrals exist. At an update $t_j$, $$\\label{eq:limit-pushforwards}\n       F_{t_j+}=(\\Theta_j)_\\#F_{t_j-},\n       \\qquad\n       J_{t_j+}=(\\Theta_j\\otimes\\Theta_j)_\\#J_{t_j-}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tree-evolution",

          "source_id": "PM-364.2"

        "exact_quote": "There is a sufficiently large dimensional integer $\\Gamma_0$ with the following property. Fix $\\Gamma\\geq\\Gamma_0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-dhm-local",
        "exact_quote": "Consider the positive kernel <a href=\"#eq:edge-integral\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:edge-integral\">[eq:edge-integral]</a> of a regular piece, with its fixed ends prescribed. Suppose the support has $|v_e|\\le |\\log\\epsilon|^{C_*}$, and each $x_e$ lies in a prescribed ball of radius $|\\log\\epsilon|^{C_*}$. The centers of those balls and all exterior variables may be arbitrary. Then:*\n\n\n1.  *A $\\{2\\}$ piece has kernel mass at most one. For a $\\{3\\}$ piece the kernel is parameterized by $$\\label{eq:local-three-parameters}\n       \\mathrm dt\\,\\mathrm d\\omega\\,\\mathrm dw\\,\n             |(v-w)\\cdot\\omega|,$$ with $(t,\\omega,w)\\in\\mathbb R\\times S^2\\times\\mathbb R^3$ and the appropriate incoming hemisphere. Here $v$ is the fixed-end velocity. If that end is outgoing, use the inverse elastic parametrization. All other states are determined by these parameters and the fixed end. A $\\{4\\}$ piece has in addition the measure $\\mu\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv$. Consequently their masses are respectively bounded by $|\\log\\epsilon|^{C_*}$ and $\\mu|\\log\\epsilon|^{C_*}$. A neutral type-A $\\{33\\}$ bound is obtained by a $\\{3\\}$ integration followed by a $\\{2\\}$ integration.*\n\n2.  *A $\\{3\\}$ piece gains $\\epsilon^\\upsilon$ over its neutral bound if its support satisfies any one of $$\\label{eq:good-three-conditions}\n     \\begin{split}\n     |t-t_*|&\\le\\epsilon^\\upsilon,\\qquad\n     |v_e-v_{e'}|\\le\\epsilon^\\upsilon,\\\\\n     |x_e-x_*|&\\le\\epsilon^{1-\\upsilon},\\qquad\n     |x_e-x_{e'}|\\le\\epsilon^{1-\\upsilon}.\n     \\end{split}$$ The two-edge conditions require distinct edges and, for O-atoms, exclude serial pairs. In the external-position condition $e$ cannot be the fixed edge, or, for an O-atom, its serial free edge. The quantities $t_*,x_*$ are fixed exterior parameters. The bound is uniform in their values.*\n\n3.  *For a type-B $\\{33\\}$ piece, both atoms are C. Suppose $|t_1-t_2|\\ge\\epsilon^\\upsilon$, and at each of its atoms every pair of distinct incident edges satisfies $|v_e-v_{e'}|\\ge\\epsilon^\\upsilon$. Its kernel mass is at most $\\epsilon^\\upsilon|\\log\\epsilon|^{C_*}$, uniformly in its two fixed ends.*\n\n*In all three assertions, multiplying by an arbitrary bounded function on the stated support costs its supremum. There is no assumption that a fixed-end velocity has an independent Gaussian distribution.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-item-cutoff-main",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:cutoff-import\" label=\"prop:cutoff-import\"></span> Let $M$ be a connected, full, realizable graph containing only scattering contacts. Full means that all its end variables are integrated. Define a transversal set $A\\subset M$ to be a set for which $M\\setminus A=A^-\\sqcup A^+$, with no directed bond from $A^+$ to $A\\cup A^-$, or from $A$ to $A^-$. Let $X_0(A)$ consist of vertices outside $A$ with two bonds to $A$, and let $X(A)$ be the closure obtained by repeatedly adding vertices with two bonds to the set already grown. The following statements hold; in parts <span class=\"upright\">(ii)–(iv)</span>, $A$ is assumed to be connected.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"item:cutoff-growth\" label=\"item:cutoff-growth\"></span> There is a chain of connected transversal sets $A_1,\\ldots,A_m=M$, with $A_1$ a tree, such that $X_0(A_j)\\neq\\varnothing$ for $j<m$, $$A_j\\cup X(A_j)\\subset A_{j+1},\\qquad\n     r(A_{j+1})=r(A_j\\cup X(A_j)),\\qquad\n     r(A_{j+1})-r(A_j)\\leq10|X(A_j)|.$$ The intermediate growth from $A_j\\cup X(A_j)$ to $A_{j+1}$ adds one bond at each new vertex. For any $D$ transitions in this chain there is a cutting sequence of all of $M$ with one type $\\{4\\}$, no type $\\{44\\}$ or type-B pair, and at least $D$ type-A pairs.*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"item:cutoff-bfk\" label=\"item:cutoff-bfk\"></span> One can choose $A^\\pm$ such that each component of $A^+$ is attached to $A$. The two parts $X^\\pm(A)=X(A)\\cap A^\\pm$ are equivalently obtained by adding vertices with two predecessors, or two successors, in the grown set. They are convex along particle lines. If $m_\\pm$ is the number of bonds from $A$ to $X^\\pm(A)$, then $$|X^\\pm(A)|\\leq G(m_\\pm),\n     \\qquad\n     G(q)=(2q+1)\\binom q2(32q^{3/2})^{q^2}\\quad(q\\geq2).$$ The harmless values $G(0),G(1)$ may be replaced by one.*\n\n3.  *<span id=\"item:cutoff-trans\" label=\"item:cutoff-trans\"></span> Put $z=|X_0^\\pm(A)|$ and $a=r(A)$. There is a cutting sequence of $M$ having one type $\\{4\\}$, no type $\\{44\\}$ or type-B pair, and at least $z/2-a-1$ type-A pairs.*\n\n4.  *For this same choice of side there is a finite Borel partition of the contact-parameter domain, with a subcase-dependent integer $y\\geq0$, and the following two available sequences in each subcase:*\n\n    - *a sequence with one type $\\{4\\}$, no type $\\{44\\}$ or type-B pair, and at least $y/10-3(a+1)$ uniformly good singletons;*\n\n    - *a sequence with no type $\\{44\\}$, at most $S=a+z+y$ type $\\{4\\}$ singletons, all type-B pairs uniformly good, and at least $$\\frac{m_\\pm-10^5S}{10}$$ type-A or type-B pairs in total.*\n\n*The singleton and pair types not counted for a gain have the neutral kernels described above. On graphs of polylogarithmic size and bounded excess the indicated partitions and operation sequences have at most $C^b|\\log\\epsilon|^{C_*}$ subcases. The exponential base $C$ depends on dimensional choices, not on $L$. The required local restrictions at each uniformly good piece involve its own variables and data exposed earlier in the cutting order.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },

Let $X_0(A)$ consist of vertices outside $A$ with two bonds to $A$, and let $X(A)$ be the closure obtained by repeatedly adding vertices with two bonds to the set already grown. The following statements hold; in parts <span class=\"upright\">(ii)–(iv)</span>, $A$ is assumed to be connected.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"item:cutoff-growth\" label=\"item:cutoff-growth\"></span> There is a chain of connected transversal sets $A_1,\\ldots,A_m=M$, with $A_1$ a tree, such that $X_0(A_j)\\neq\\varnothing$ for $j<m$, $$A_j\\cup X(A_j)\\subset A_{j+1},\\qquad\n     r(A_{j+1})=r(A_j\\cup X(A_j)),\\qquad\n     r(A_{j+1})-r(A_j)\\leq10|X(A_j)|.$$ The intermediate growth from $A_j\\cup X(A_j)$ to $A_{j+1}$ adds one bond at each new vertex. For any $D$ transitions in this chain there is a cutting sequence of all of $M$ with one type $\\{4\\}$, no type $\\{44\\}$ or type-B pair, and at least $D$ type-A pairs.*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"item:cutoff-bfk\" label=\"item:cutoff-bfk\"></span> One can choose $A^\\pm$ such that each component of $A^+$ is attached to $A$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-cutoff-neutral",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose an exposed set is order-convex in the directed contact graph. Every remaining component touching this set can be cut using only type $\\{2\\}$ and type $\\{3\\}$ singletons. The same conclusion holds with given top ends in place of the exposed set. Scattering and nonscattering contacts are both allowed.",
