    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cutoff-pair",
        "exact_quote": "Consider every possible choice $e_1,e_2$ of the two prospective fixed edges of a type-A pair, with $t_1,t_2$ the corresponding contact times. Call that choice strongly close if $$\\label{eq:cutoff-close}\n |t_2-t_1|\\leq\\epsilon^{9/10},\\qquad\n |v_{e_2}-v_{e_1}|\\leq\\epsilon^{4/5}.$$ Then:*\n\n\n1.  *a primitive pair satisfying <a href=\"#eq:cutoff-close\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cutoff-close\">[eq:cutoff-close]</a>, cut freely as one piece, has mass at most $$\\label{eq:cutoff-free-pair}\n     C R^C\\mu\\epsilon^{9/10+3(4/5)}\n     =C R^C\\epsilon^{13/10};$$*\n\n2.  *in the absence of every strongly close primitive pair, each type-A pair used by a cutting sequence has conditional mass at most $C R^C\\epsilon^{1/20}$, or may be split into a neutral type-two piece and a singleton with this same improved bound.*\n\n*The conditional bounds are uniform in the previously exposed lines and times.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cutoff-pair",
          "exact_quote": "Consider every possible choice $e_1,e_2$ of the two prospective fixed edges of a type-A pair, with $t_1,t_2$ the corresponding contact times. Call that choice strongly close if $$\\label{eq:cutoff-close}\n |t_2-t_1|\\leq\\epsilon^{9/10},\\qquad\n |v_{e_2}-v_{e_1}|\\leq\\epsilon^{4/5}.$$ Then:*\n\n\n1.  *a primitive pair satisfying <a href=\"#eq:cutoff-close\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cutoff-close\">[eq:cutoff-close]</a>, cut freely as one piece, has mass at most $$\\label{eq:cutoff-free-pair}\n     C R^C\\mu\\epsilon^{9/10+3(4/5)}\n     =C R^C\\epsilon^{13/10};$$*\n\n2.  *in the absence of every strongly close primitive pair, each type-A pair used by a cutting sequence has conditional mass at most $C R^C\\epsilon^{1/20}$, or may be split into a neutral type-two piece and a singleton with this same improved bound.*\n\n*The conditional bounds are uniform in the previously exposed lines and times.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Consider every possible choice $e_1,e_2$ of the two prospective fixed edges of a type-A pair, with $t_1,t_2$ the corresponding contact times. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Spatially summable Gaussian Bol norm** ([source](#eq:bol-norm)): For $\beta>0$, define $$\label{eq:bol-norm} \\|g\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta} :=\sum_{a\in\mathbb Z^3} \sup_{\substack{\|x-a\|\le1\\v\in\mathbb R^3}} e^{\beta\|v\|^2}\|g(x,v)\|.$$
- **Grand-canonical hard-sphere ensemble** ([source](#eq:grand-canonical)): The initial probability law, including $N=0$, is $$\label{eq:grand-canonical} \mathbb P_\epsilon(N,\mathrm dZ_N) =\mathcal Z_\epsilon^{-1}\frac{\mu^N}{N!} \mathbf 1_{\mathcal D_N^\epsilon}(Z_N) \prod_{i=1}^Nf_0(z_i)\,\mathrm dz_i.$$
- **Exact-centered empirical fluctuation** ([source](#eq:empirical-fluctuation)): For real $\phi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^6)$, set $$\label{eq:empirical-fluctuation} \pi_t^\epsilon(\phi)=\mu^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^N\phi(z_i(t)), \qquad \zeta_t^\epsilon(\phi) =\sqrt\mu\,[\pi_t^\epsilon(\phi)-\mathbb E_\epsilon\pi_t^\epsilon(\phi)].$$
- **Normalized initial factorial cumulants** ([source](#eq:initial-partition-definition)): The normalized factorial cumulant densities, also called connected densities, $c_{h,0}^{\epsilon}$ are defined by $$\label{eq:initial-partition-definition} F_{h,0}^{\epsilon}(Z_h) =\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal P([h])} \prod_{B\in\pi}c_{\|B\|,0}^{\epsilon}(Z_B).$$
- **Layered collision and encounter history** ([source](#stmt-def-dhm-history)): A history consists of finitely many oriented particle lines and binary atoms, with an order on the atoms along every line.
- **Scattering and straight-continuation atoms** ([source](#stmt-def-dhm-history)): A C-atom applies elastic scattering, and an O-atom records an incoming encounter with straight continuation.
- **Pasted dynamics** ([source](#pm-h-11)): We call the evolution obtained by concatenating these restarted slab dynamics the pasted dynamics.
- **Merger and redundant contact** ([source](#pm-h-23)): A contact is a *merger* if its two lines belong to different components immediately before that contact; otherwise call it redundant.
- **Accumulated observation mark** ([source](#eq:limit-mark-update)): Give every particle a scalar mark, initially $y=0$, and at time $t_j$ perform the update $$\label{eq:limit-mark-update} (x,v,y)\longmapsto \Theta_j(x,v,y) =(x,v,y+\phi_j(x,v)).$$
- **Covariance including the same-particle term** ([source](#pm-h-32)): Restore the same-particle term by defining $$\label{eq:limit-G-definition} G_t(\Phi,\Psi)=J_t(\Phi\otimes\Psi)+F_t(\Phi\Psi).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| quantity-microscopic-activity | Microscopic activity | = | $\epsilon^{-2}$ | Sphere diameter tends to zero. |
| quantity-initial-link-exponent | Coarse initial-link exponent | = | $1/81$ | Fixed exponent independent of root order. |
| quantity-initial-integrated-cumulants | Initial connected-density integral norm | ≤ | $C_h\epsilon^{3(h-1)}$ | Fixed root order with $h\ge2$. |
| quantity-slab-duration | Slab width | = | $T/L$ | The number of slabs is fixed before the diameter limit. |
| quantity-particle-size-cutoff | Slab particle cap | = | $A_i^{30}$ | The cutoff hierarchy depends on diameter and fixed slab count. |
| quantity-one-root-kinetic-error | One-root Gaussian Bol error | ≤ | $\epsilon^{\theta_i}$ | Full slabs and their partial-slab versions. |
| quantity-stopped-variation-error | Stopped hierarchy total variation error | ≤ | $e^{-c_i\Lambda_i}$ | the final slab may be partial. |
| quantity-sharp-connected-scale | Connected factorial cumulant total variation | ≤ | $C_h\mu^{1-h}$ | Fixed bounded observation list and fixed factorial order. |
| quantity-diagram-net-scaling | Activity and positional merger scaling | = | $\mu^{c-h}\epsilon^p$ | Component count, designated roots, and physical initial links. |
| quantity-uniform-size-majorant | Scaled connected diagram measure | ≤ | $C_K(1+n)^{d_K}(C\tau\sqrt L)^n$ | Slab sizes with total size $n$; the base is independent of complexity. |
| quantity-higher-normalized-cumulants | Higher ordinary cumulants of normalized marked observations | = | $O_k(\mu^{1-k/2})$ | Fixed $k>2$ as diameter tends to zero. |
| quantity-dynamical-discrepancy | Probability of any true-pasted trajectory discrepancy | ≤ | $C\epsilon^{1+\eta}$ | Fixed kinetic data, excess cap, and slab count. |
| quantity-discrepancy-exponent | Admissible discrepancy exponent | = | $1/20$ | Sufficiently small sphere diameter. |
| quantity-primitive-pair-gain | Freely cut strongly close primitive-pair mass | ≤ | $C R^C\epsilon^{13/10}$ | Strong time and velocity closeness with polylogarithmic localization. |
| quantity-gain-activity-balance | Retained gain minus free-singleton activity loss | ≥ | $6/5$ | Nonprimitive-seed branch of the finite decomposition. |
| quantity-causal-time-speed-majorant | Slab time-order volume times relative-speed product | ≤ | $\frac{\tau^{q_i}(2q_iP)^{q_i/2}}{q_i!}$ | An empty slab contributes the factor one. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-initial-ensemble** [claim](#stmt-prop-initial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-initial): Insertion bounds, connected Mayer expansion, tree partition, cumulant convergence, and excluded-volume expansion. -> Initial factorial bounds, particle-number moments, Gaussian fluctuations, and centering shift.
- **step-global-moments** [claim](#stmt-lem-target-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-target-moments): Positive gain-series majorization with Gaussian exponent losses. -> Global weighted spatial-velocity integrability.
- **step-backward-evolution** [claim](#stmt-lem-target-propagator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-target-propagator): Convergent time-ordered mild series with distributed exponent allowance. -> Unique backward propagator and upper-end identity.
- **step-gaussian-field** [claim](#stmt-prop-gaussian-target) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gaussian-target): Independent isonormal Gaussian maps and backward covariance representation. -> Well-defined Gaussian target and uniqueness in its second-moment class.
- **step-kinetic-envelopes** [claim](#stmt-lem-kinetic-envelopes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kinetic-envelopes): Imported propagation estimate with decreasing Gaussian exponent. -> Uniform summable Gaussian envelopes for density and spatial gradient.
- **step-layered-package** [claim](#stmt-thm-dhm-package) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-dhm-package): Imported positive history estimates, spatial envelope decomposition, and signed tree comparison. -> Coarse complexity bounds and full or partial signed-tree kinetic approximation.
- **step-boundary-modifications** [claim](#stmt-lem-dhm-dummies) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dhm-dummies): Root-deficit estimates and positive artificial-root or dummy-line insertion. -> Permitted normalization losses for missing continuations and unobserved terminals.
- **step-autonomous-partition** [claim](#stmt-lem-autonomous-partition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-autonomous-partition): Chronological uniqueness of juxtaposed compatible autonomous histories. -> Partition identity and exact root-history observation decomposition.
- **step-rooted-forest** [claim](#stmt-lem-rooted-forest) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rooted-forest): Deterministic pair exploration with forest stopping and connected-graph grouping. -> Retained block identity, stopped forest domination, and Ursell tree bound.
- **step-slab-operator** [claim](#stmt-lem-one-slab-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-one-slab-expansion): Janossy summation, spectator cancellation, and fresh-label factorial counting. -> Exact retained slab operator plus dominated variation remainder.
- **step-one-root-bridge** [claim](#stmt-lem-one-root-bridge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-one-root-bridge): Refine autonomous clusters into ordered molecules and identify discrepancy by extra encounters. -> Leading signed coefficient and positive domination of bridge errors.
- **step-independent-factors** [claim](#stmt-prop-one-root) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-one-root): Slab induction using fixed-endpoint bridge bounds and kinetic comparison. -> Bol-bounded independent density representatives and kinetic closeness.
- **step-record-regrouping** [claim](#stmt-lem-marked-regrouping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-regrouping): Finite dependent-subset expansion and resummation of unmarked one-root blocks. -> Exact dependent-subset decomposition and record projection compatibility.
- **step-label-components** [claim](#stmt-lem-diagram-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-diagram-labels): Distribute fresh labels among components and recover partitions from ordered C/O graphs. -> Connected-component inversion and factorial denominator preservation.
- **step-variation-induction** [claim](#stmt-lem-stopped-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stopped-variation): Positive stopped-history estimates and crude operator control of preceding variation errors. -> Exponentially small unweighted retained hierarchy error.
- **step-marked-expansion** [claim](#stmt-thm-marked-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-marked-expansion): Finite factorial inversion and coarse geometric summation of high complexity. -> Actual cumulants as connected retained diagrams with removable high complexity.
- **step-terminal-witness** [claim](#stmt-cor-terminal-witness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-terminal-witness): Insert the preceding retained hierarchy into a positive terminal-witness factorial functional. -> Attached lower-layer expansion preserving witness restrictions.
- **step-merger-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-lem-sharp-merger-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sharp-merger-coordinates): Chronological relative-translation changes with a triangular Jacobian. -> Incoming merger flux factors and redundant-contact indicators.
- **step-sharp-factorials** [claim](#stmt-lem-sharp-factorials) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sharp-factorials): Bound distinguished labels and redundant contacts polynomially while retaining fresh-label denominators. -> Factorial majorant with complexity-independent ordinary exponential base.
- **step-sharp-summability** [claim](#stmt-lem-sharp-summability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sharp-summability): Joint energy-bin, pair-flux, time-simplex, and factorial estimates. -> Uniform absolute size summability and Gaussian or spatially fixed variants.
- **step-extra-contact-nullsets** [claim](#stmt-lem-sharp-no-extra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sharp-no-extra): Analytic nonidentity of collinearity constraints and uniform finite-tree trajectory convergence. -> Almost-everywhere absence of additional incoming contacts at small diameter.
- **step-spatial-traces** [claim](#stmt-lem-sharp-spatial-traces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sharp-spatial-traces): Use a designated position as representative translation and pass through uniformly summable tails. -> Continuous spatial conditional measures and well-defined common-position products.
- **step-tree-completeness** [claim](#stmt-lem-sharp-tree-completeness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sharp-tree-completeness): Downward dependent-component induction and interface incidence forest counting. -> Bounded complexity for all leading trees and preserved record projections.
- **step-last-contact-splitting** [claim](#stmt-lem-sharp-tree-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sharp-tree-splitting): Finite labelled tree deletion and joining followed by absolute size-tail removal. -> Exact component products at last contacts with inherited factorial weights.
- **step-sharp-cumulants** [claim](#stmt-thm-sharp-cumulants) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sharp-tree-splitting-2): Remove high complexity, apply merger scaling, eliminate initial links and cycles, and pass to complete tree limits. -> Sharp cumulant scale and limiting mean and connected-pair tree measures.
- **step-marked-tree-evolution** [claim](#stmt-prop-tree-evolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tree-evolution): Cancel irrelevant last-contact C/O choices and split contacts incident to tested roots. -> Transport mild marked evolution and update pushforwards.
- **step-pasted-gaussian-convergence** [claim](#stmt-prop-cumulant-clt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cumulant-clt): Restore the diagonal covariance, propagate backward pulses, and use factorial-to-ordinary cumulant identities. -> Gaussian observation limit and all centered scalar moments on the pasted dynamics.
- **step-energy-localization** [claim](#stmt-lem-cutoff-energy-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff-energy-tail): Crude forward incoming-contact integration and a reserved Gaussian birth-energy tail. -> Negligible very-high-energy contribution.
- **step-local-kernels** [claim](#stmt-lem-dhm-local) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dhm-local): Imported coarea bounds for regular localized pieces with fixed exterior data. -> Uniform neutral and improved conditional elementary bounds.
- **step-transversal-alternatives** [claim](#stmt-item-cutoff-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-item-cutoff-main): Imported realizable collision-graph growth and transversal cutting sequences. -> Available singleton and pair gain counts with controlled free pieces.
- **step-neutral-completion** [claim](#stmt-lem-cutoff-neutral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff-neutral): Expose minimal successors or maximal predecessors while preserving order convexity. -> Completion by neutral singleton cuts without another free seed.
- **step-adjacent-pairs** [claim](#stmt-lem-cutoff-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff-pair): Separate primitive closeness, aiming-tube restrictions, and exposed intermediate-time restrictions. -> Improved primitive mass or uniform conditional type-A gain.
- **step-amplified-gains** [claim](#stmt-lem-cutoff-amplified) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff-amplified): Choose a finite dimensional threshold hierarchy and combine transversal alternatives with pair gains. -> A primitive seed or gain balance paying all free-singleton activity costs.
- **step-ordinary-extraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-cutoff-extraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff-extraction): Positive kernel Fubini and backward exceptional-kernel integration with all ordinary variables fixed. -> Exceptional mass product times literal ordinary parameter volume.
- **step-common-velocity-volume** [claim](#stmt-lem-cutoff-ellipsoid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff-ellipsoid): Chronological orthogonal propagation, exceptional resets, finite nets, and ellipsoid volume estimates. -> Common containing velocity set independent of numerical contact times.
- **step-causal-time-volume** [claim](#stmt-lem-cutoff-time) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff-time): Enumerate linear extensions with weighted antichain energy bounds. -> Per-slab time-volume and relative-speed bound.
- **step-cut-integral-summation** [claim](#stmt-lem-cutoff-sum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff-sum): Combine spatial cells, dyadic energy bins, ordinary parameter extraction, common velocity volumes, and causal time bounds. -> Geometric ordinary-contact contraction retaining exceptional diameter gains.
- **step-cutoff-probability** [claim](#stmt-prop-cutoff) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff-sum-2): Count first-disagreement terminal witnesses and sum excess and size branches separately. -> Trajectory discrepancy probability below the fluctuation scale.
- **step-centering-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-center-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-center-transfer): Coupling and Hölder comparison using preserved particle count and initial moments. -> Negligible exact-expectation difference and matching centered weak limits.
- **step-main-limit** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-center-transfer-2): Accumulate each real observation combination in records, transfer its centered limit, and apply Cramér–Wold. -> True hard-sphere finite-dimensional fluctuation theorem.

          "exact_quote": "The retained hierarchy admits an exact finite expansion $$\\label{eq:dependent-subsets}\n \\widehat F^R_{i,S}\n   =\\sum_{H:\\,R\\subseteq H\\subseteq S}\n        E^R_{i,H}\\otimes g_i^{\\otimes(S\\setminus H)}.$$ The coefficients can be represented by layered diagrams with the following rule. Every retained block in slab $i$ contains a root from the interface above it. A block is retained in $E^R_{i,H}$ if it contains at least two such roots, or it meets a dependent input label from the interface below. All other blocks sum to the independent factor $g_i$ on their unique root. Every record-bearing line continues to time zero. Forgetting any record commutes with <a href=\"#eq:retained-hierarchy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:retained-hierarchy\">[eq:retained-hierarchy]</a>; at the diagram level it is effected by resumming the newly unmarked one-root histories.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-record-regrouping",
      "inputs": [
        "Initial cumulant blocks",
        "Finite retained slab operators",
        "Required record labels"
      ],
      "method": "Finite dependent-subset expansion and resummation of unmarked one-root blocks.",
      "output": "Exact dependent-subset decomposition and record projection compatibility.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-marked-regrouping"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-diagram-labels",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-record-regrouping"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-diagram-labels",
          "exact_quote": "The retained expansion factors over its global components. If $D^R_{i,B}$ is the sum of its diagrams connected on the designated roots $B$, then $$\\label{eq:component-exponential-formula}\n \\widehat F^R_{i,S}\n       =\\sum_{\\pi\\in\\mathcal P(S)}\n                         \\bigotimes_{B\\in\\pi}D^{R\\cap B}_{i,B}.$$ Consequently factorial-cumulant inversion of the retained hierarchy keeps exactly its connected diagrams. This assertion is unchanged by the resummations into $g_i$.*\n\n\n*At fixed root order $h=|S|$ and bounded DHM complexity $\\rho\\le K$, let $n_i$ denote the number of particle lines in slab $i$, and $j_i$ its fresh input labels. The labelled denominator is $\\prod_i j_i!$, where $$\\label{eq:bounded-crossing-factorials}\n n_i-j_i=O_{K,h,m,L}(1),\\qquad\n \\prod_i\\frac1{j_i!}\n      \\le\\frac{(1+\\sum_i n_i)^{C_{K,h,m,L}}}{\\prod_i n_i!}.$$ Apart from the choices of ordinary contact pairs and their order, all distinguished-label, gluing, initial-block, and record-placement multiplicities are bounded by a fixed polynomial in total size times $C^b$, where $b$ is the atom count and the exponential base $C$ does not depend on $K$ or the moment order. In the coarse regime the same choices cost a power of the cutoffs with exponent $O(\\rho)$, in addition to the exponential atom factor.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-label-components",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite marked retained expansion",
        "Fresh-label factorial coefficients"
      ],
      "method": "Distribute fresh labels among components and recover partitions from ordered C/O graphs.",
      "output": "Connected-component inversion and factorial denominator preservation.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-diagram-labels"
    },

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-stopped-variation",
          "exact_quote": "Choose $L$ sufficiently large in terms of the fixed data and $\\Gamma$. The statement is uniform for a partial final slab. No velocity weight is claimed for this error.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-stopped-variation"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-marked-expansion",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-label-components",
        "step-variation-induction",
        "step-layered-package"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-marked-expansion",
          "exact_quote": "Choose $\\Gamma$ and then $L$ as above. These choices are independent of the moment order and the fixed observation list. On this single pasted dynamics the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *The factorial hierarchy with any fixed finite list of records has the finite componentwise expansion <a href=\"#eq:retained-hierarchy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:retained-hierarchy\">[eq:retained-hierarchy]</a>, with the variation error <a href=\"#eq:retained-variation-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:retained-variation-error\">[eq:retained-variation-error]</a>. Its independent inputs are the $g_i$ in [prop:one-root](#prop:one-root); its label weights, continuations, and record projections are those of [lem:marked-regrouping](#lem:marked-regrouping), [lem:diagram-labels](#lem:diagram-labels).*\n\n2.  *At each fixed final order $h$, the actual normalized marked factorial cumulant $c_h^\\epsilon$ is the sum of retained globally connected diagrams, plus an error of total variation at most $C_h e^{-c\\Lambda_L}$. A nontrivial initial cumulant block is part of this connectivity. Dummy links are not.*\n\n3.  *For any fixed accuracy power $P$, one can choose a constant $K$ after the observation list and $h$ such that all remaining retained diagrams with $\\rho>K$ have summed absolute variation at most $\\epsilon^P$. At $\\rho\\le K$ the original fresh-label factorials satisfy <a href=\"#eq:bounded-crossing-factorials\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:bounded-crossing-factorials\">[eq:bounded-crossing-factorials]</a>. Neither increasing $K$ nor increasing a fixed moment order changes the exponential base per ordinary atom in the preceding estimates.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-marked-expansion",
      "inputs": [

        "Stopped variation bound",
        "Positive complexity package"

      "method": "Finite factorial inversion and coarse geometric summation of high complexity.",
      "output": "Actual cumulants as connected retained diagrams with removable high complexity.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-thm-marked-expansion"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-cor-terminal-witness",
          "exact_quote": "In slab $i$, let a terminal witness be a connected true C history on $n\\le3\\Lambda_i$ incoming labels, of excess at most $2\\Gamma$, stopped at a contact time in that slab. It may carry arbitrary further nonnegative realizability restrictions. Sum its indicator with the full activity normalization $$\\label{eq:terminal-witness-functional}\n \\sum_n\\frac{\\mu^n}{n!}\n       \\int F_{i-1,n}(\\mathrm dZ_n)\\,\n                       \\mathbf 1_{\\text{terminal witness}}(Z_n).$$ For the terminal classes in (ii), this expression has the preceding retained layer expansion, with independent $g$ inputs and initial blocks. All lower C/O components attach through particle lines to the terminal witness. The preceding variation error is smaller than every fixed power of $\\epsilon$. After the artificial-top-root normalization cost $\\mu$, the coarse part with $\\rho>K$ can also be made smaller than any assigned power by choosing $K$ sufficiently large. The remaining low-complexity witness integrals retain their realizability restrictions.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-cor-terminal-witness"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-merger-coordinates",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a finite line graph, the C/O choices, the chronological order, and which contacts are redundant in that order. Away from nontransversal contacts and simultaneous events, its history integral has coordinates consisting of the birth velocities, one absolute translation for each global component, a vector in a ball of radius $R_{K,D}\\epsilon$ for each physical initial merger, and $(t,\\omega)\\in\\mathbb R\\times S^2$ for each dynamic merger. The positional Jacobian at a dynamic merger is $$\\label{eq:sharp-jacobian}\n       \\epsilon^2[(v-v_*)\\cdot\\omega]_+\\,\\mathrm dt\\,\\mathrm d\\omega.$$ A specified redundant contact has no free continuous parameter and requires no extra Jacobian. The coordinate map has multiplicity at most one on each prescribed incoming straight-segment branch.",

        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-no-extra",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-no-extra",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a finite chronological forest with no physical initial links, with a prescribed C/O choice on each edge, on its incoming merger branches. Outside a null set of its merger parameters the following hold.*\n\n1.  *All specified contacts occur at distinct times away from interfaces and observation times and are transversal.*\n\n2.  *Before the first redundant contact, the demand for such a contact is false for all sufficiently small $\\epsilon$.*\n\n3.  *On a full tree, an additional contact not in the prescribed list, including a contact preceding the prescribed joining of its two groups, is absent for all sufficiently small $\\epsilon$.*\n\n*In (ii), the independent parameters used are those of the forest prefix; arbitrary remaining merger parameters may be appended. In (iii), an outgoing crossing following an O contact is not an additional contact, because only incoming crossings are counted.",

    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-spatial-traces",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-sharp-summability",
        "step-extra-contact-nullsets"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-spatial-traces",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal T$ be any of the absolutely summable tree measures just constructed, and designate a line and a time at which its position is required. It has a version of the disintegration $$\\label{eq:sharp-disintegration}\n               \\mathcal T=\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathcal T_x$$ with respect to that position. For a sufficiently small $\\alpha>0$, the Gaussian-weighted variation of $\\mathcal T_x$ is bounded uniformly in $x$; its integral over $x$ is finite. For every bounded continuous test of the other coordinates, and also for continuous tests of smaller Gaussian growth, $x\\mapsto\\mathcal T_x(\\Phi)$ is continuous.*\n\n*If two separate tree components are joined at their designated positions, the product of their conditional measures at a common position, integrated against incoming flux, is an absolutely defined measure. It is the result of imposing the joining position in the merger coordinates. Equivalently it is obtained by a smooth approximate identity for equality of the two positions. These facts apply to the marked one-root and connected two-root tree measures and their record projections.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-tree-splitting",
          "exact_quote": "In the leading tree sums, deleting a last contact of the highest slab that meets a designated root splits its global tree into the product of the two component tree measures, with the incident lines designated before that contact. Conversely, joining such two disjoint component histories at that contact gives exactly the corresponding retained tree, with its original factorial weight.     {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-sharp-cumulants",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-marked-expansion",
        "step-merger-coordinates",

        "step-extra-contact-nullsets",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-sharp-cumulants",
          "exact_quote": "Choose the parameters $\\Gamma,L$ as in [thm:marked-expansion](#thm:marked-expansion), increasing $L$, if necessary, by an amount depending only on the uniform bounds for the Boltzmann solution and on $T$. This one choice works for every fixed factorial order and every fixed bounded observation list. For each fixed $h$, $$\\label{eq:sharp-full-cumulant}\n \\|c_h^\\epsilon\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\le C_h\\mu^{1-h}.$$ For $h=1,2$, the measures $\\mu^{h-1}c_h^\\epsilon$ converge against bounded continuous record tests to the signed sums of the retained global trees with trivial initial cumulants. At a contact their weight is the incoming flux $[(v-v_*)\\cdot\\omega]_+\\,\\mathrm dt\\,\\mathrm d\\omega$; a scattering contact has sign $+1$, and a non-scattering contact has sign $-1$. The independent birth factors are $f_{i\\tau}$.*\n\n*The sums of the absolute retained diagram measures, after multiplication by $\\mu^{h-1}$, are bounded uniformly with a sufficiently small Gaussian weight in their designated endpoint velocities. The same bound holds for the limiting tree measures, and for the Gaussian weights in finitely many retained record velocities after decreasing the exponent. Among the retained connected diagrams, the sum of the absolute measures having a nontrivial initial cumulant or a global cycle is $o(\\mu^{1-h})$. The assertions about Gaussian weights concern retained diagrams and their limits; no weighted estimate for a microscopic expansion remainder is asserted.",

        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-tree-evolution",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tree-evolution",
          "exact_quote": "Between observation updates the limiting measures satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\partial_tF_t(\\Phi)\n  &=F_t(D\\Phi)+\\frac12\\int BF_tF_{t,*}\\Delta\\Phi,\n       \\label{eq:limit-F-evolution}\\\\\n \\partial_tJ_t(\\Phi\\otimes\\Psi)\n  &=J_t\\bigl((D+H_t)\\Phi\\otimes\\Psi\n                     +\\Phi\\otimes(D+H_t)\\Psi\\bigr)\\notag\\\\\n  &\\quad+\\int BF_tF_{t,*}\n          \\bigl(\\Phi(\\xi')\\Psi(\\xi_*')\n                         -\\Phi(\\xi)\\Psi(\\xi_*)\\bigr).\n       \\label{eq:limit-J-evolution}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The identities hold in transport mild form, and hence in weak differential form for smooth tests for which the displayed integrals exist. At an update $t_j$, $$\\label{eq:limit-pushforwards}\n       F_{t_j+}=(\\Theta_j)_\\#F_{t_j-},\n       \\qquad\n       J_{t_j+}=(\\Theta_j\\otimes\\Theta_j)_\\#J_{t_j-}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }

      "output": "Transport mild marked evolution and update pushforwards.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-tree-evolution"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-cutoff-energy-tail",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-terminal-witness",
        "step-independent-factors"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cutoff-energy-tail",
          "exact_quote": "For the positive graph majorants in <a href=\"#eq:cutoff-low-complexity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cutoff-low-complexity\">[eq:cutoff-low-complexity]</a>, there is a fixed power $H_*$ such that restricting the total birth energy $P=\\sum_{j\\text{ a birth}}|v_j|^2$ to $$P\\leq P_{\\epsilon}:=(1+|\\log\\epsilon|)^{H_*}$$ changes their sum by $O(\\epsilon^M)$ for every fixed $M$.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-energy-localization",
      "inputs": [
        "Positive low-complexity witness graphs",
        "Polylogarithmic graph size",
        "Common birth envelopes"
      ],
      "method": "Crude forward incoming-contact integration and a reserved Gaussian birth-energy tail.",
      "output": "Negligible very-high-energy contribution.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-cutoff-energy-tail"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-dhm-local",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-dhm-local",
          "exact_quote": "Consider the positive kernel <a href=\"#eq:edge-integral\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:edge-integral\">[eq:edge-integral]</a> of a regular piece, with its fixed ends prescribed. Suppose the support has $|v_e|\\le |\\log\\epsilon|^{C_*}$, and each $x_e$ lies in a prescribed ball of radius $|\\log\\epsilon|^{C_*}$. The centers of those balls and all exterior variables may be arbitrary. Then:*\n\n\n1.  *A $\\{2\\}$ piece has kernel mass at most one. For a $\\{3\\}$ piece the kernel is parameterized by $$\\label{eq:local-three-parameters}\n       \\mathrm dt\\,\\mathrm d\\omega\\,\\mathrm dw\\,\n             |(v-w)\\cdot\\omega|,$$ with $(t,\\omega,w)\\in\\mathbb R\\times S^2\\times\\mathbb R^3$ and the appropriate incoming hemisphere. Here $v$ is the fixed-end velocity. If that end is outgoing, use the inverse elastic parametrization. All other states are determined by these parameters and the fixed end. A $\\{4\\}$ piece has in addition the measure $\\mu\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv$. Consequently their masses are respectively bounded by $|\\log\\epsilon|^{C_*}$ and $\\mu|\\log\\epsilon|^{C_*}$. A neutral type-A $\\{33\\}$ bound is obtained by a $\\{3\\}$ integration followed by a $\\{2\\}$ integration.*\n\n2.  *A $\\{3\\}$ piece gains $\\epsilon^\\upsilon$ over its neutral bound if its support satisfies any one of $$\\label{eq:good-three-conditions}\n     \\begin{split}\n     |t-t_*|&\\le\\epsilon^\\upsilon,\\qquad\n     |v_e-v_{e'}|\\le\\epsilon^\\upsilon,\\\\\n     |x_e-x_*|&\\le\\epsilon^{1-\\upsilon},\\qquad\n     |x_e-x_{e'}|\\le\\epsilon^{1-\\upsilon}.\n     \\end{split}$$ The two-edge conditions require distinct edges and, for O-atoms, exclude serial pairs. In the external-position condition $e$ cannot be the fixed edge, or, for an O-atom, its serial free edge. The quantities $t_*,x_*$ are fixed exterior parameters. The bound is uniform in their values.*\n\n3.  *For a type-B $\\{33\\}$ piece, both atoms are C. Suppose $|t_1-t_2|\\ge\\epsilon^\\upsilon$, and at each of its atoms every pair of distinct incident edges satisfies $|v_e-v_{e'}|\\ge\\epsilon^\\upsilon$. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-regular-interval-gaussian-limit**: The exact-centered empirical fluctuations converge jointly at fixed observation times to the specified Gaussian generalized field on the assumed regular kinetic interval.
- **invariant-exact-microscopic-centering**: The microscopic expectation supplies the centering; replacing it by the Boltzmann density can retain a deterministic excluded-volume shift.
- **invariant-finite-connected-tree-bound**: For Boolean pair incompatibilities on a finite vertex set, the absolute signed connected-graph coefficient is bounded by the active spanning-tree count, with the prescribed singleton convention.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-kinetic-centering-substitution**: Center observations by the classical Boltzmann density instead of the exact microscopic expectation. -> The excluded-volume correction can survive the fluctuation scaling as a deterministic shift.
- **anti-path-space-upgrade**: Use the finite-dimensional limit as a certificate of convergence for trajectory extrema or other path-dependent functionals. -> The conclusion supplies no path-space tightness theorem.
- **anti-random-collision-model-transfer**: Apply the hard-sphere fluctuation theorem directly to a simulator that resamples collisions. -> A random collision rule changes the microscopic dynamics from which the limiting covariance is derived.
- **anti-finite-algebra-certifies-kinetic-limit**: Treat successful finite graph checks as verification of the Gaussian kinetic limit or the complete stopped-forest expansion. -> The executable checks only the finite connected coefficient and spanning-tree inequality; it does not check history integrals, analytic limits, centering transfer, or the stopped remainder.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The rooted forest stopping lemma explicitly contains finite Boolean connected-graph algebra and a spanning-tree bound. This implementation checks that finite subresult, including its singleton convention. The main Gaussian fluctuation limit, history-integral estimates, and stopped-forest remainder are outside executable coverage.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON containing n and either edges or all_graphs=true; name is optional metadata.
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= n <= 5; exhaustive mode requires n <= 4; edges are distinct unordered pairs of distinct vertices in range.
# OUTPUTS: JSON rows containing the active-edge mask, signed connected coefficient, and spanning-tree count.
# TIME: Finite exhaustive enumeration of edge subsets; exhaustive mode also enumerates active graphs.
# SPACE: Edge lists, traversal sets, and the finite output rows.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python integers.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception; no numerical approximation is used.
import itertools
import json
import sys

def connected(n, chosen):
    adjacency = [set() for _ in range(n)]
    for a, b in chosen:
        adjacency[a].add(b)
        adjacency[b].add(a)
    seen = {0}
    stack = [0]
    while stack:
        a = stack.pop()
        for b in adjacency[a] - seen:
            seen.add(b)
            stack.append(b)
    return len(seen) == n

def evaluate(n, pairs, active_mask):
    active = [e for j, e in enumerate(pairs) if active_mask & (1 << j)]
    coefficient = 0
    trees = 0
    for subset_mask in range(1 << len(active)):
        chosen = [e for j, e in enumerate(active) if subset_mask & (1 << j)]
        if connected(n, chosen):
            coefficient += (-1) ** len(chosen)
            if len(chosen) == n - 1:
                trees += 1
    return {'mask': active_mask, 'connected': coefficient, 'trees': trees}

def main():
    data = json.load(sys.stdin)
    n = data['n']
    if type(n) is not int or not 1 <= n <= 5:
        raise ValueError('invalid vertex count')
    pairs = list(itertools.combinations(range(n), 2))
    exhaustive = data.get('all_graphs', False)
    if type(exhaustive) is not bool:
        raise ValueError('invalid exhaustive flag')
    if exhaustive:
        if n > 4 or 'edges' in data:
            raise ValueError('invalid exhaustive request')
        masks = range(1 << len(pairs))
    else:
        normalized = []
        for edge in data['edges']:
            if not isinstance(edge, list) or len(edge) != 2:
                raise ValueError('invalid edge')
            a, b = edge
            if type(a) is not int or type(b) is not int or not 0 <= a < n or not 0 <= b < n or a == b:
                raise ValueError('invalid endpoint')
            normalized.append(tuple(sorted((a, b))))
        if len(set(normalized)) != len(normalized):
            raise ValueError('duplicate edge')
        indices = {e: j for j, e in enumerate(pairs)}
        masks = [sum(1 << indices[e] for e in normalized)]
    rows = [evaluate(n, pairs, mask) for mask in masks]
    print(json.dumps({'rows': rows}, sort_keys=True))

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

            "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a finite vertex set, let $R\\subseteq W$ be nonempty, and attach $a_e\\in\\{0,1\\}$ to each unordered pair of vertices. Fix $M\\ge2$ and set $$\\label{eq:ursell-block}\n u(B)=\\sum_{\\substack{G\\text{ connected}\\\\V(G)=B}}\n             \\prod_{e\\in E(G)}(-a_e),\\qquad u(\\{w\\})=1.$$ There is a finite signed remainder $\\mathcal R$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{eq:finite-rooted-identity}\n \\prod_{e\\subseteq W}(1-a_e)\n &=\\sum_{I:\\,R\\subseteq I\\subseteq W}\n   \\sum_{\\substack{\\mathcal B\\in\\mathcal P(I)\\\\\n               B\\cap R\\ne\\varnothing,\\ |B|<M\\ (B\\in\\mathcal B)}}\n       \\prod_{B\\in\\mathcal B}u(B)\n       \\prod_{e\\subseteq W\\setminus I}(1-a_e)+\\mathcal R.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Its absolute value is bounded by a sum of rooted forests. Each forest in this bound has exactly one component with $M\\le |B|\\le2M-2$; its other components have fewer than $M$ vertices. All these components meet $R$. Vertices outside the forest retain their full mutual compatibility product. The coefficient of each selected forest in this domination is at most one. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:ursell-tree-bound}\n |u(B)|\\le\\sum_{T\\text{ spanning tree on }B}\\prod_{e\\in E(T)}a_e.$$",

    "oracle_code": "import itertools\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom functools import lru_cache\nfrom sympy import Matrix\n\ndef independent(n, pairs, active_mask):\n    active = [e for j, e in enumerate(pairs) if active_mask & (1 << j)]\n    full = (1 << n) - 1\n    def compatibility(vertices):\n        return int(not any((vertices & (1 << a)) and (vertices & (1 << b)) for a, b in active))\n    @lru_cache(None)\n    def coefficient(vertices):\n        if vertices == 0:\n            raise ValueError('empty connected block')\n        first = vertices & -vertices\n        result = compatibility(vertices)\n        block = (vertices - 1) & vertices\n        while block:\n            if block & first:\n                result -= coefficient(block) * compatibility(vertices ^ block)\n            block = (block - 1) & vertices\n        return result\n    laplacian = [[0 for _ in range(n)] for _ in range(n)]\n    for a, b in active:\n        laplacian[a][a] += 1\n        laplacian[b][b] += 1\n        laplacian[a][b] -= 1\n        laplacian[b][a] -= 1\n    trees = 1 if n == 1 else int(Matrix([row[:-1] for row in laplacian[:-1]]).det())\n    value = coefficient(full)\n '__main__':\n    try:\n        sys.exit(main())\n    except Exception:\n        sys.exit(1)\n",
    "output_contract": "The candidate emits a JSON object with rows in active-mask order. Each row contains exactly mask, connected, and trees, all exact integers. connected is the alternating sum over connected spanning subgraphs. trees counts active spanning trees, including the singleton convention. The expected token VALID instructs the independent oracle to check the complete output and the tree inequality; it is not candidate stdout.",

This implementation checks that finite subresult, including its singleton convention. The main Gaussian fluctuation limit, history-integral estimates, and stopped-forest remainder are outside executable coverage.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON containing n and either edges or all_graphs=true; name is optional metadata.\n# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= n <= 5; exhaustive mode requires n <= 4; edges are distinct unordered pairs of distinct vertices in range.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON rows containing the active-edge mask, signed connected coefficient, and spanning-tree count.\n# TIME: Finite exhaustive enumeration of edge subsets; exhaustive mode also enumerates active graphs.\n# SPACE: Edge lists, traversal sets, and the finite output rows.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python integers.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception; no numerical approximation is used.\nimport itertools\nimport json\nimport sys\n\ndef connected(n, chosen):\n    adjacency = [set() for _ in range(n)]\n    for a, b in chosen:\n        adjacency[a].add(b)\n        adjacency[b].add(a)\n    seen = {0}\n    stack = [0]\n    while stack:\n        a = stack.pop()\n        for b in adjacency[a] - seen:\n            seen.add(b)\n            stack.append(b)\n    return len(seen) == n\n\ndef evaluate(n, pairs, active_mask):\n    active = [e for j, e in enumerate(pairs) if active_mask & (1 << j)]\n    coefficient = 0\n    trees = 0\n    for subset_mask in range(1 << len(active)):\n        chosen = [e for j, e in enumerate(active) if subset_mask & (1 << j)]\n        if connected(n, chosen):\n            coefficient += (-1) ** len(chosen)\n            if len(chosen) == n - 1:\n                trees += 1\n    return {'mask': active_mask, 'connected': coefficient, 'trees': trees}\n\ndef main():\n    data = json.load(sys.stdin)\n    n = data['n']\n    if type(n) is not int or not 1 <= n <= 5:\n        raise ValueError('invalid vertex count')\n    pairs = list(itertools.combinations(range(n), 2))\n    exhaustive = data.get('all_graphs', False)\n    if type(exhaustive) is not bool:\n        raise ValueError('invalid exhaustive flag')\n    if exhaustive:\n        if n > 4 or 'edges' in data:\n            raise ValueError('invalid exhaustive request')\n        masks = range(1 << len(pairs))\n    else:\n        normalized = []\n        for edge in data['edges']:\n            if not isinstance(edge, list) or len(edge) != 2:\n                raise ValueError('invalid edge')\n            a, b = edge\n            if type(a) is not int or type(b) is not int or not 0 <= a < n or not 0 <= b < n or a == b:\n                raise ValueError('invalid endpoint')\n            normalized.append(tuple(sorted((a, b))))\n        if len(set(normalized)) != len(normalized):\n            raise ValueError('duplicate edge')\n        indices = {e: j for j, e in enumerate(pairs)}\n        masks = [sum(1 << indices[e] for e in normalized)]\n    rows = [evaluate(n, pairs, mask) for mask in masks]\n    print(json.dumps({'rows': rows}, sort_keys=True))\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The deterministic rooted-forest exploration supplies a concrete procedure for generating retained branches and stopped remainders from a finite incompatibility graph. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The restarted truncated dynamics gives a concrete finite-particle collision rule that can be adapted for hard-sphere simulation. Its fluctuation comparison remains conditional on the kinetic assumptions and the asymptotic regime. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The positive stopped expression supplies a mathematical domination of a signed remainder, rather than an executable certification tool for arbitrary particle data. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The finite connected-graph partition explicitly constructs spanning-tree classes and their compatibility factors, allowing exact evaluation of connected coefficients for Boolean edge data. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Layered histories define a concrete graph representation with serial particle lines, ordered scattering and encounter atoms, slab membership, designated roots, and observation records. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The simultaneous velocity containing-set argument bounds a geometric integration domain; it supplies no accelerator mapping, arithmetic architecture, or hardware execution contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Gaussian collision noise is derived from deterministic mechanical trajectories and an initial probability law. No cryptographic construction or adversarial security property is established. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Observation marks accumulate prescribed test values along particle trajectories. They introduce neither a learning rule nor a statistical guarantee for trained models. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Minimum spanning trees partition connected graph terms in the proof. The paper does not formulate an operational objective or prove optimality for a scheduling or allocation problem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Component products result from the labelled history expansion and its factorial weights. They do not establish independence, consistency, or coordination guarantees for distributed processes. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The principal application is analytical kinetic fluctuation theory. Its Gaussian field construction and finite-dimensional limit do not supply a finite numerical solver or a path-space simulation contract. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-regular-kinetic-background** (hypothesis): The fluctuation theorem assumes a smooth initial probability density with spatially summable Gaussian bounds on the density and its spatial gradient, together with a nonnegative classical Boltzmann solution retaining uniform Gaussian decay on the prescribed finite interval. Blocked use: ['Apply the theorem after loss of the assumed classical regularity or Gaussian bound.', 'Treat the fluctuation theorem as a proof of global Boltzmann regularity.']
- **limit-finite-dimensional-observations** (formal_scope): The main convergence concerns fixed finite lists of observation times and smooth compactly supported real tests. It does not establish convergence of full fluctuation trajectories. Blocked use: ['Infer convergence of trajectory extrema or other path-dependent observables.', 'Claim path-space tightness from the finite-dimensional theorem.']
- **limit-deterministic-hard-sphere-model** (formal_scope): The microscopic law uses deterministic elastic hard-sphere collisions and free flight. Random collision rules and resampling are outside this model. Blocked use: ['Transfer the covariance theorem directly to resampled collision dynamics.', 'Apply the hard-sphere result to other interaction laws without an independent argument.']
- **limit-grand-canonical-covariance** (formal_scope): The initial particle count is random. The limiting initial covariance therefore retains the same-particle contribution without a mean-product subtraction. Blocked use: ['Substitute a fixed-particle-count initial covariance.', 'Interpret the stated grand-canonical covariance as a conditioned particle-count result.']
- **limit-exact-microscopic-centering** (approximation): The excluded-volume correction to the initial mean can survive fluctuation scaling. Centering only by the Boltzmann density generally introduces a deterministic shift. Blocked use: ['Replace exact microscopic expectation by a law-of-large-numbers approximation.', 'Report a centered Gaussian limit after removing only the kinetic mean.']
- **limit-gaussian-field-test-space-sense** (formal_scope): The Gaussian target is constructed as a mean-square linear field on the stated Gaussian-growth test scales. This does not assert samplewise membership in their Banach dual. Blocked use: ['Treat each realization as a bounded functional on the full Banach test space.', 'Infer a sample-path regularity theorem from second-moment operator bounds.']
- **limit-backward-exponent-allowance** (hypothesis): Backward propagation requires a positive allowance between the input and output Gaussian exponents. Uniqueness is asserted in the stated mild scales, and Gaussian-field uniqueness uses uniformly bounded second-moment operator norms. Blocked use: ['Assert the same propagator estimate without Gaussian exponent loss.', 'Extend uniqueness to arbitrary generalized solutions without the stipulated second-moment bounds.']
- **limit-global-integrability** (hypothesis): The noise construction needs global spatial-velocity integrability, rather than only a spatially uniform Gaussian velocity bound. The paper obtains that integrability from the initial spatial summability and the kinetic evolution. Blocked use: ['Construct the whole-space noise covariance using only a uniform pointwise envelope.', 'Discard initial spatial summability while retaining the same global covariance claim.']
- **limit-restarted-dynamics-and-parameter-order** (hypothesis): The pasted dynamics concatenates slabs whose collision prehistories restart. The excess cap and slab count are fixed before the observation list and moment order; later complexity truncations do not redefine the random dynamics. Blocked use: ['Retain collision prehistory across interfaces and identify that evolution with the pasted dynamics.', 'Choose a different pasted process for each requested moment order.']
- **limit-complete-signed-one-root-comparison** (approximation): The imported kinetic comparison concerns the specified complete signed tree sum. Identifying the retained one-root operator with that sum requires the separate bridge argument and its positive cycle, size, and extra-encounter errors. Blocked use: ['Apply the signed-tree kinetic estimate to an arbitrary one-root operator.', 'Infer fixed-endpoint Gaussian Bol control from an integrated mass estimate alone.']
- **limit-positive-input-and-stopped-domination** (hypothesis): The one-slab remainder domination requires a positive input point-process law and finite factorial measures through the specified stopped input order. The positive stopped expression dominates the signed remainder; it is not its asserted local kernel. Blocked use: ['Apply the same domination directly to arbitrary signed input hierarchies.', 'Replace the signed remainder by its positive majorant as an equality.', 'Omit spectator compatibility factors during the finite stopped-branch identity.']
- **limit-unweighted-microscopic-remainder** (formal_scope): Stopped hierarchy errors have unweighted total variation control. Gaussian velocity weights are established for retained diagrams and limiting tree measures, without a weighted estimate for the microscopic expansion remainder. Blocked use: ['Assign Gaussian velocity-weighted control to the actual expansion error.', 'Transfer convergence to arbitrary unbounded record tests using only the remainder estimate.']
- **limit-fixed-order-and-record-list** (formal_scope): Sharp cumulant bounds apply at each fixed factorial order and for each fixed bounded observation list. Their front constants and polynomial degrees may depend on these fixed choices. Blocked use: ['Claim uniform cumulant control when factorial order grows with the diameter limit.', 'Allow an increasing observation list without separately controlling its constants and Gaussian exponent losses.']
- **limit-finite-tree-nullset-threshold** (nonconstructive): Extra-contact exclusion holds outside a null set for fixed finite forests and sufficiently small diameter. The cycle argument uses finite-size dominated convergence followed by removal of summable tails, without a rate uniform in the number of vertices. Blocked use: ['Extract a universal finite-diameter exclusion threshold for all histories.', 'Use finite graph samples to certify disappearance of every redundant-contact contribution.']
- **limit-spatial-joining-products** (hypothesis): Joining separate tree components at a collision position uses the continuous conditional versions and absolute integrability established by spatial disintegration. It is not an arbitrary product of unspecified almost-everywhere conditional measures. Blocked use: ['Multiply arbitrary representatives of spatial disintegrations at a common point.', 'Interpret limiting tree products as independence of the actual particle law at an interface.']
- **limit-cutoff-realizability-and-local-support** (hypothesis): The imported pure-collision cutting alternatives require connected, full, realizable scattering graphs. Conditional local gains also require regular pieces, localized support, and every stated closeness or nondegeneracy restriction. Blocked use: ['Apply the realizable collision-count bound to an arbitrary abstract graph.', 'Retain a local gain after dropping its producing support restriction.', 'Assume fixed-end velocities have independent Gaussian laws.']
- **limit-primitive-pair-and-gain-balance** (hypothesis): The freely cut close-pair estimate requires a primitive adjacent scattering pair with the specified time and velocity closeness. The amplified alternative applies within its stated excess range and pays free-singleton activity losses using retained improved pieces. Blocked use: ['Assign the freely cut primitive-pair bound to every adjacent pair.', 'Count improved local gains without subtracting free-component activity costs.', 'Use the amplified cutting conclusion outside its prescribed excess range.']
- **limit-common-energy-and-causal-projection** (hypothesis): Ordinary-parameter extraction retains gain-producing exceptional restrictions and requires a measurable containing projection. The common velocity volume and time-speed bounds refer to original feasible histories, their shared birth-energy budget, and the causal order inherited through exceptional atoms. Blocked use: ['Condition exceptional kernel bounds on the global energy event without justification.', 'Replace the common velocity containing set by unrelated velocity boxes and preserve the claimed summation bound.', 'Discard causal chains through exceptional atoms before estimating ordinary time volume.']
- **limit-centering-transfer-strength** (approximation): Transferring exact expectations at fluctuation scale uses the stronger discrepancy probability together with preserved particle count and sufficiently high fixed particle-number moments. A discrepancy probability merely tending to zero does not supply that expectation comparison. Blocked use: ['Transfer exact centering from an unspecified vanishing discrepancy probability.', 'Claim convergence of all centered moments for the true dynamics solely from the weak-limit transfer lemma.']
- **limit-finite-oracle-coverage** (formal_scope): Finite Boolean connected-graph algebra is a constructive subresult. Its verification does not establish the complete stopped-forest remainder, continuous history integrals, the Gaussian kinetic limit, or exact-centering transfer. Blocked use: ['Promote a finite tree-inequality receipt into certification of the full fluctuation theorem.', 'Claim that bounded graph enumeration decides the general analytic limit.']

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-initial",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently small $\\epsilon$, the following assertions hold.*\n\n\n\n1.  *The factorial densities satisfy $$\\label{eq:initial-factorial-upper}\n     0\\le F_{h,0}^{\\epsilon}\\le\\prod_{j=1}^h f_0(z_j),\n     \\qquad (1-C\\epsilon)f_0\\le g_0\\le f_0.$$ Consequently $\\|g_0-f_0\\|_{\\mathrm{Bol},2\\beta}=O(\\epsilon)$ and, for every fixed integer $p\\ge1$, $$\\label{eq:initial-N-moments}\n     \\mathbb E(N)_p\\le\\mu^p,\n     \\qquad \\sup_{0<\\epsilon\\le\\epsilon_0}\\mathbb E(N/\\mu)^p<\\infty.$$*\n\n\n\n\n2.  *With $\\upsilon=1/81$, for every $h\\ge2$, $$\\label{eq:initial-coarse-links}\n     |c_{h,0}^{\\epsilon}(Z_h)|\n     \\le (Ch)^{Ch}\\prod_{j=1}^h f_0(z_j)\n       \\sum_{\\mathcal T\\text{ tree on }[h]}\n         \\prod_{\\{i,j\\}\\in\\mathcal T}\n           \\bigl(\\mathbf 1_{|x_i-x_j|\\le\\epsilon}+\\epsilon^\\upsilon\\bigr).$$ Here $C$ and the upper bound on $\\epsilon$ are independent of $h$. For fixed $h\\ge2$ and $D>0$ there is $C_{D,h}<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{eq:initial-sharp-links}\n     |c_{h,0}^{\\epsilon}(Z_h)|\n     \\le C_{D,h}\\prod_{j=1}^h f_0(z_j)\n       \\sum_{\\mathcal T\\text{ tree on }[h]}\n         \\prod_{\\{i,j\\}\\in\\mathcal T}\n           \\bigl(\\mathbf 1_{|x_i-x_j|\\le C_{D,h}\\epsilon}+\\epsilon^D\\bigr).$$ Moreover, $$\\label{eq:initial-integrated-cumulants}\n     \\|c_{h,0}^{\\epsilon}\\|_{L^1}\\le C_h\\epsilon^{3(h-1)}\n     \\qquad(h\\ge2).$$*\n\n\n3.  *For every finite list of bounded real tests, the exact-centered initial fluctuations converge jointly to a centered Gaussian vector with covariance $\\int f_0\\phi\\psi$. For a smooth compactly supported real test $\\phi$, one also has $$\\label{eq:initial-centering-shift}\n     \\lim_{\\epsilon\\to0}\\sqrt\\mu\n     \\left(\\mathbb E\\pi_0^\\epsilon(\\phi)-\\int f_0\\phi\\right)\n     =-\\frac{4\\pi}{3}\\int_{\\mathbb R^6}\n           f_0(x,v)\\phi(x,v)\\varrho_0(x)\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-target-propagator",
          "exact_quote": "For $0\\le a<b<2\\beta$ and $0\\le r\\le t\\le T$, there is a bounded backward propagator $U(r,t):E_a^0\\to E_b^0$. It solves $$\\label{eq:target-backward-mild}\n U(r,t)h=\\mathsf S_{t-r}h\n   +\\int_r^t\\mathsf S_{s-r}K_sU(s,t)h\\,\\mathrm ds,$$ has the composition law with intermediate exponents, and is the unique mild propagator in these scales. For $\\phi\\in C_c^\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:target-upper-end-identity}\n U(r,t)\\phi-\\phi=\\int_r^t U(r,s)A_s\\phi\\,\\mathrm ds.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-gaussian-target",
          "exact_quote": "The fluctuation equation in [thm:main](#thm:main) has a centered Gaussian solution on the test scales $E_a^0$, $0\\le a<\\beta$. Its covariance is $$\\label{eq:target-covariance}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathbb E[\\zeta_t(\\phi)\\zeta_s(\\psi)]\n ={}&\\int f_0(z)\\,U(0,t)\\phi(z)\\,U(0,s)\\psi(z)\\,\\mathrm dz\\\\\n &+\\int_0^{\\min(s,t)}\n     C_r\\bigl(U(r,t)\\phi,U(r,s)\\psi\\bigr)\\,\\mathrm dr.\n \\end{split}$$ It is unique in the class of solutions extending to these scales with uniformly bounded second-moment operator norms. In particular, the law required in [thm:main](#thm:main) is well defined.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }

          "exact_quote": "Choose $\\Gamma$ and then $L$ as above. These choices are independent of the moment order and the fixed observation list.           "exact_quote": "Assume [ass:kinetic](#ass:kinetic), the parameter hierarchy <a href=\"#eq:dhm-cutoffs\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dhm-cutoffs\">[eq:dhm-cutoffs]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:dhm-exponents\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dhm-exponents\">[eq:dhm-exponents]</a>, and a common bound on the Gaussian Bol norms of all birth inputs. The birth inputs need not equal the approximate density constructed in the source, but must satisfy its common Gaussian Bol envelope.*\n\n\n\n2.  *Suppose the independent input $g$ in slab $i$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:dhm-input-closeness}\n     \\|g-f_{(i-1)\\tau}\\|_{\\mathrm{Bol},\\beta_{i-1}}\n           \\le\\epsilon^{\\theta_{i-1}}.$$ Let $\\mathsf T_i^\\epsilon[g]$ be the leading signed sum just defined. Then $$\\label{eq:dhm-signed-trees}\n     \\|\\mathsf T_i^\\epsilon[g]-f_{i\\tau}\\|_{\\mathrm{Bol},\\beta_i}\n           \\le\\tfrac12\\epsilon^{\\theta_i}.$$ In the fixed-top-endpoint norm $\\mathrm{Bol},\\widetilde\\beta_i$, the summed absolute contributions of cyclic members of $\\mathcal T_i$, members of $\\mathcal T_i^{\\mathrm{err}}$, and tree members of $\\mathcal T_i$ with at least $\\Lambda_i/3$ lines are $o(\\epsilon^{\\theta_i})$. With the same cutoffs and exponent budgets, write $\\mathsf T_{i,\\sigma}^\\epsilon$ for its signed tree sum. Then $$\\label{eq:dhm-partial-slab}\n \\sup_{0\\le\\sigma\\le\\tau}\n \\|\\mathsf T_{i,\\sigma}^\\epsilon[g]\n          -f_{(i-1)\\tau+\\sigma}\\|_{\\mathrm{Bol},\\beta_i}\n       \\le\\tfrac12\\epsilon^{\\theta_i},$$ and the positive error classes obey the same full-$\\tau$ upper majorants uniformly in $\\sigma$.           "exact_quote": "For a positive input point-process law, let $F'$ be its factorial hierarchy after the truncated slab.         },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rooted-forest",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a finite vertex set, let $R\\subseteq W$ be nonempty, and attach $a_e\\in\\{0,1\\}$ to each unordered pair of vertices. Fix $M\\ge2$ and set $$\\label{eq:ursell-block}\n u(B)=\\sum_{\\substack{G\\text{ connected}\\\\V(G)=B}}\n             \\prod_{e\\in E(G)}(-a_e),\\qquad u(\\{w\\})=1.$$ There is a finite signed remainder $\\mathcal R$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{eq:finite-rooted-identity}\n \\prod_{e\\subseteq W}(1-a_e)\n &=\\sum_{I:\\,R\\subseteq I\\subseteq W}\n   \\sum_{\\substack{\\mathcal B\\in\\mathcal P(I)\\\\\n               B\\cap R\\ne\\varnothing,\\ |B|<M\\ (B\\in\\mathcal B)}}\n       \\prod_{B\\in\\mathcal B}u(B)\n       \\prod_{e\\subseteq W\\setminus I}(1-a_e)+\\mathcal R.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Its absolute value is bounded by a sum of rooted forests. Each forest in this bound has exactly one component with $M\\le |B|\\le2M-2$; its other components have fewer than $M$ vertices. All these components meet $R$. Vertices outside the forest retain their full mutual compatibility product. The coefficient of each selected forest in this domination is at most one. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:ursell-tree-bound}\n |u(B)|\\le\\sum_{T\\text{ spanning tree on }B}\\prod_{e\\in E(T)}a_e.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        }
      ],

At a contact their weight is the incoming flux $[(v-v_*)\\cdot\\omega]_+\\,\\mathrm dt\\,\\mathrm d\\omega$; a scattering contact has sign $+1$, and a non-scattering contact has sign $-1$.           "anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-spatial-traces",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal T$ be any of the absolutely summable tree measures just constructed, and designate a line and a time at which its position is required. It has a version of the disintegration $$\\label{eq:sharp-disintegration}\n               \\mathcal T=\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathcal T_x$$ with respect to that position. For a sufficiently small $\\alpha>0$, the Gaussian-weighted variation of $\\mathcal T_x$ is bounded uniformly in $x$; its integral over $x$ is finite. For every bounded continuous test of the other coordinates, and also for continuous tests of smaller Gaussian growth, $x\\mapsto\\mathcal T_x(\\Phi)$ is continuous.*\n\n*If two separate tree components are joined at their designated positions, the product of their conditional measures at a common position, integrated against incoming flux, is an absolutely defined measure. These facts apply to the marked one-root and connected two-root tree measures and their record projections.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sharp-tree-splitting",
          "exact_quote": "In the leading tree sums, deleting a last contact of the highest slab that meets a designated root splits its global tree into the product of the two component tree measures, with the incident lines designated before that contact. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove a finite-dimensional central limit theorem away from equilibrium for a deterministic grand-canonical hard-sphere gas in three dimensions. The initial probability density is smooth, with spatially summable Gaussian velocity bounds on the density and its spatial gradient. The limit holds on every finite interval on which the classical Boltzmann solution has uniform Gaussian velocity decay. The empirical measure is centered by its exact microscopic expectation. Its fluctuations converge to the Gaussian solution of the linear fluctuating Boltzmann equation.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction and statement of the theorem

The Boltzmann equation describes the leading empirical distribution of a dilute gas. The next-order question asks whether the small correlations created by deterministic collisions produce Gaussian fluctuations, and which equation governs their covariance. We prove that the finite-dimensional fluctuation law persists throughout an assumed regular kinetic interval, without an equilibrium or small-data hypothesis.

Lanford’s derivation of the Boltzmann equation established the kinetic limit for short times (Lanford 1975) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Lanford1975](#ref-Lanford1975)]. The next-order problem has a separate history. Van Beijeren, Lanford, Lebowitz and Spohn derived short-time equilibrium fluctuation covariances governed by the linearized Boltzmann equation (Beijeren et al. 1980) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[vanBeijerenLanfordLebowitzSpohn1980](#ref-vanBeijerenLanfordLebowitzSpohn1980)], and Spohn established the nonequilibrium second-moment limit (Spohn 1981) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Spohn1981](#ref-Spohn1981)]. These covariance results identify the candidate fluctuation dynamics; a Gaussian limit also requires control of higher-order correlations.

Pulvirenti and Simonella developed short-time quantitative correlation-error estimates and a cumulant-type reorganization of collision histories (Pulvirenti and Simonella 2017) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[PulvirentiSimonella2017](#ref-PulvirentiSimonella2017)]. Bodineau, Gallagher, Saint-Raymond and Simonella developed the short-time nonequilibrium fluctuation theory (Bodineau et al. 2020, 2023b) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BGSS2020](#ref-BGSS2020), [BGSS2023](#ref-BGSS2023)], including convergence of the fluctuation process. Their connected-cumulant analysis identifies the Gaussian collision noise and uses exact microscopic centering. Their direct cluster expansion on physical trajectories (Bodineau et al. 2022) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BGSSCluster2022](#ref-BGSSCluster2022)] provides a complementary representation of the short-time statistical dynamics. At equilibrium, they obtained long-time covariance convergence (Bodineau et al. 2023a) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BGSSCorrelations2023](#ref-BGSSCorrelations2023)] and then the full Gaussian fluctuation limit (Bodineau et al. 2024) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BGSS2024](#ref-BGSS2024)], using the invariant measure. The BGSS fluctuation theorems concern a spatially periodic gas; the present setting is the whole space, with integrable initial data. Our conclusion extends the time interval away from equilibrium but concerns finite-dimensional laws, rather than process convergence.

Deng, Hani and Ma extended the hard-sphere kinetic derivation through each prescribed interval on which the regular Boltzmann solution exists with the required bounds (Deng, Hani, and Ma 2025) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[DHM2025](#ref-DHM2025)]. They also identify long-time nonequilibrium fluctuations as a further application of their approach (Deng, Hani, and Ma 2025, sec. 1.4.3) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[DHM2025](#ref-DHM2025)]. The theorem below establishes the finite-dimensional nonequilibrium fluctuation statement on that regular kinetic lifespan. Its proof retains their layered collision histories and supplies the sharper connected estimates and centering transfer required at the fluctuation scale. It does not assert global regularity for the Boltzmann equation or a path-space tightness theorem.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Kinetic equation and normalization

<a id="eq:bol-norm"></a>
<a id="eq:collision-kernel"></a>
<a id="eq:boltzmann"></a>
Phase space is $\mathbb R_x^3\times\mathbb R_v^3$, and $z=(x,v)$. For $\beta>0$, define $$\label{eq:bol-norm}
 \|g\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta}
 :=\sum_{a\in\mathbb Z^3}
   \sup_{\substack{|x-a|\le1\\v\in\mathbb R^3}}
    e^{\beta|v|^2}|g(x,v)|.$$ The collision convention is <a id="eq:scattering"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 B(v-v_*,\omega)&=[(v-v_*)\cdot\omega]_+,
   &&\omega\in\mathbb S^2,\label{eq:collision-kernel}\\
 v'&=v-[(v-v_*)\cdot\omega]\omega,
 &v_*'&=v_*+[(v-v_*)\cdot\omega]\omega.\label{eq:scattering}
\end{aligned}$$ Here $\mathrm d\omega$ is the usual unnormalized surface measure, and $[a]_+=\max\{a,0\}$. With all densities in the collision integral evaluated at the same $x$, set $$\label{eq:boltzmann}
 (\partial_t+v\cdot\nabla_x)f=Q(f,f),\qquad
 Q(f,f)=\int_{\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb S^2}
 B(v-v_*,\omega)(f'f_*'-ff_*)\,\mathrm dv_*\,\mathrm d\omega.$$

<a id="stmt-ass-kinetic"></a>
<div id="ass:kinetic" class="assumption">

<a id="eq:initial-bol-bounds"></a>
<a id="eq:kinetic-gaussian"></a>
**Assumption 1**. The function $f_0\ge0$ is a smooth probability density and, for some $\beta>0$, $$\label{eq:initial-bol-bounds}
 \|f_0\|_{\mathrm{Bol},2\beta}
 +\|\nabla_xf_0\|_{\mathrm{Bol},2\beta}<\infty.$$ There is a nonnegative classical solution $f$ of <a href="#eq:boltzmann" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:boltzmann">[eq:boltzmann]</a> with initial datum $f_0$ on a prescribed finite interval $[0,T]$, $T>0$, such that $$\label{eq:kinetic-gaussian}
 M:=\sup_{0\le t\le T}\sup_{x,v}
       e^{2\beta|v|^2}f(t,x,v)<\infty.$$

</div>

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Microscopic law and fluctuation field

<a id="eq:grand-canonical"></a>
For sphere diameter $\epsilon>0$, let $\mu=\epsilon^{-2}$, and write $$\mathcal D_N^\epsilon
 =\{(z_1,\ldots,z_N):|x_i-x_j|>\epsilon\text{ for }i\ne j\}.$$ The initial probability law, including $N=0$, is $$\label{eq:grand-canonical}
 \mathbb P_\epsilon(N,\mathrm dZ_N)
 =\mathcal Z_\epsilon^{-1}\frac{\mu^N}{N!}
   \mathbf 1_{\mathcal D_N^\epsilon}(Z_N)
   \prod_{i=1}^Nf_0(z_i)\,\mathrm dz_i.$$ The normalizing constant satisfies $1\le\mathcal Z_\epsilon\le e^\mu$. Conditional on the initial configuration, particles move by free flight and deterministic elastic hard-sphere collisions. The almost-everywhere definition of that flow is given by (Deng, Hani, and Ma 2025, Proposition 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[DHM2025](#ref-DHM2025)]; its exceptional set has zero probability under the initial law. There is no random collision rule or resampling.

<a id="eq:empirical-fluctuation"></a>
For real $\phi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^6)$, set $$\label{eq:empirical-fluctuation}
 \pi_t^\epsilon(\phi)=\mu^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^N\phi(z_i(t)),
 \qquad
 \zeta_t^\epsilon(\phi)
 =\sqrt\mu\,[\pi_t^\epsilon(\phi)-\mathbb E_\epsilon\pi_t^\epsilon(\phi)].$$ The expectation in <a href="#eq:empirical-fluctuation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:empirical-fluctuation">[eq:empirical-fluctuation]</a> is exact. A law-of-large-numbers approximation to it need not be accurate on this scale; even the first excluded-volume correction at time zero can survive after multiplication by $\sqrt\mu$.

<a id="eq:noise-form"></a>
For an unmarked test $\phi$, use the pair difference $$\Delta\phi
 =\phi(x,v')+\phi(x,v_*')-\phi(x,v)-\phi(x,v_*).$$ The limiting weak drift and collision covariance are <a id="eq:drift"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 (A_t\phi)(x,v)
 &=v\cdot\nabla_x\phi(x,v)
   +\int_{\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb S^2}
      B(v-v_*,\omega)f_t(x,v_*)\Delta\phi
          \,\mathrm dv_*\,\mathrm d\omega,\label{eq:drift}\\
 C_t(\phi,\psi)
 &=\frac12\int_{\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb S^2}
       B(v-v_*,\omega)f_t(x,v)f_t(x,v_*)
       \Delta\phi\,\Delta\psi
       \,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm dv_*\,\mathrm d\omega.\label{eq:noise-form}
\end{aligned}$$

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:initial-covariance"></a>
<a id="eq:main-convergence"></a>
**Theorem 2** (Finite-dimensional fluctuation limit). *Under [ass:kinetic](#ass:kinetic), the centered Gaussian generalized field $\zeta$ with weak equation <a id="eq:fluctuating-equation"></a>$$\label{eq:fluctuating-equation}
 \mathrm d\zeta_t(\phi)=\zeta_t(A_t\phi)\,\mathrm dt+\mathrm dW_t(\phi)$$ is well defined by its backward mild form on a scale of tests with small Gaussian growth. Its initial field is independent of $W$, and <a id="eq:noise-covariance"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \mathbb E[\zeta_0(\phi)\zeta_0(\psi)]
   &=\int_{\mathbb R^6}f_0\phi\psi\,\mathrm dz,\label{eq:initial-covariance}\\
 \mathbb E[W_t(\phi)W_s(\psi)]
   &=\int_0^{t\wedge s}C_r(\phi,\psi)\,\mathrm dr.\label{eq:noise-covariance}
\end{aligned}$$ The Gaussian noise $W$ has independent increments. For every integer $m\ge1$, fixed times $t_1,\ldots,t_m\in[0,T]$, and real tests $\phi_1,\ldots,\phi_m\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^6)$, $$\label{eq:main-convergence}
 \bigl(\zeta_{t_1}^\epsilon(\phi_1),\ldots,
       \zeta_{t_m}^\epsilon(\phi_m)\bigr)
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \bigl(\zeta_{t_1}(\phi_1),\ldots,
       \zeta_{t_m}(\phi_m)\bigr)
 \qquad(\epsilon\downarrow0).$$*

</div>

The initial covariance in <a href="#eq:initial-covariance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:initial-covariance">[eq:initial-covariance]</a> has no subtraction of $(\int f_0\phi)(\int f_0\psi)$: the particle count in <a href="#eq:grand-canonical" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:grand-canonical">[eq:grand-canonical]</a> is random. The equivalent backward covariance formula and the precise construction of the Gaussian field are given in [prop:gaussian-target](#prop:gaussian-target).

There is also a concrete distinction between microscopic and kinetic centering. Put $\varrho_0(x)=\int f_0(x,v)\,\mathrm dv$. The initial ensemble calculation in [prop:initial](#prop:initial) gives $$\lim_{\epsilon\to0}\sqrt\mu
 \left(\mathbb E\pi_0^\epsilon(\phi)-\int f_0\phi\right)
 =-\frac{4\pi}{3}\int f_0(x,v)\phi(x,v)\varrho_0(x)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv.$$ Thus exclusion changes the mean at exactly the fluctuation scale, even though its contribution to the limiting initial covariance vanishes. Centering by the Boltzmann density alone would generally introduce a nonzero deterministic shift already at time zero.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof structure and new estimates

The proof retains the layered collision-history framework of (Deng, Hani, and Ma 2025) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[DHM2025](#ref-DHM2025)]; its operational inputs and their hypotheses are stated in [thm:dhm-package](#thm:dhm-package). The connected graph bounds also use the classical tree-graph partition scheme of Penrose (Penrose 1967) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Penrose1967](#ref-Penrose1967)]; we include its proof for the initial ensemble. The fluctuation argument needs two improvements beyond coarse decorrelation.

First, factorial cumulant measures on $h$ observed particle histories must be controlled at the sharp scale $\mu^{1-h}$. We obtain an exact component expansion that keeps finitely many observation records and preserves factorial cancellation. After coarse bounds remove histories of large complexity, chronological relative translations give one $\epsilon^2$ factor for every merger. Initial excluded-volume links gain an additional $\epsilon$, while a first redundant contact disappears on each finite tree. A summable bound, uniform in the permitted history sizes, justifies the passage from these finite histories to the full expansion. This is [thm:sharp-cumulants](#thm:sharp-cumulants).

The surviving signed trees determine both one-particle means and the connected two-particle contribution. Adding the diagonal term produces <a href="#eq:noise-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:noise-form">[eq:noise-form]</a>. Higher ordinary cumulants vanish after central-limit scaling. A mark that accumulates observations along each particle reduces every fixed multitime linear combination to this same calculation.
