Its variance is <a href="#eq:limit-variance-formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-variance-formula">[eq:limit-variance-formula]</a>, hence is the variance specified by <a href="#eq:target-covariance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:target-covariance">[eq:target-covariance]</a>. Since this holds for every real choice of coefficients, the Cramér–Wold theorem proves <a href="#eq:main-convergence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main-convergence">[eq:main-convergence]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-44"></a>
#### References

<div id="refs" class="references csl-bib-body hanging-indent">

<div id="ref-vanBeijerenLanfordLebowitzSpohn1980" class="csl-entry">

Beijeren, H. van, Oscar E. Lanford III, Joel L. Lebowitz, and Herbert Spohn. 1980. “Equilibrium Time Correlation Functions in the Low-Density Limit.” *Journal of Statistical Physics* 22 (2): 237–57. <https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01008050>.

</div>

<div id="ref-BGSS2020" class="csl-entry">

Bodineau, Thierry, Isabelle Gallagher, Laure Saint-Raymond, and Sergio Simonella. 2020. “Fluctuation Theory in the Boltzmann–Grad Limit.” *Journal of Statistical Physics* 180: 873–95. <https://doi.org/10.1007/s10955-020-02549-5>.

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "Chronological connectivity for four particle lines. Each solid contact joins two previously separate components and fixes one relative translation, with the incoming-flux Jacobian $ϵ^{2}B dt dω$. The dashed contact is internal to the already joined component. Its relative position is fixed, so it contributes a constraint rather than a new translation variable. Later common translations cannot change that constraint. Edges depict the contact graph, not physical trajectories.",
      "figure_id": "fig:chronological-mergers",
      "pdf_page": 26,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:chronological-mergers",
          "exact_quote": "Chronological connectivity for four particle lines. Each solid\ncontact joins two previously separate components and fixes one relative\ntranslation, with the incoming-flux Jacobian \\(\\eps^2B\\,\\dd t\\,\\dd\\omega\\).\nThe dashed contact is interna",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,\n  particle/.style={circle,draw=black,fill=white,minimum size=6mm,inner sep=1pt},\n  merger/.style={thick},\n  extra/.style={thick,dashed,draw=linkblue},\n  lab/.style={font=\\small,fill=white,inner sep=2pt}]\n \\node[particle] (a) at (0,0) {$a$};\n \\node[particle] (b) at (1.6,1) {$b$};\n \\node[particle] (c) at (3.2,1) {$c$};\n \\node[particle] (d) at (4.8,0) {$d$};\n \\draw[merger] (a)--node[lab,above left] {$t_1$}(b);\n \\draw[merger] (b)--node[lab,above] {$t_3$}(c);\n \\draw[merger] (c)--node[lab,above right] {$t_2$}(d);\n \\draw[extra] (a) to[bend right=24]\n    node[lab,below] {$t_4$: redundant}(d);\n \\node[anchor=west,font=\\small,align=left] at (6.1,.95)\n   {$t_1:\\ \\{a\\}\\cup\\{b\\}\\longrightarrow\\{a,b\\}$\\\\[5pt]\n    $t_2:\\ \\{c\\}\\cup\\{d\\}\\longrightarrow\\{c,d\\}$\\\\[5pt]\n    $t_3:\\ \\{a,b\\}\\cup\\{c,d\\}\\longrightarrow\\{a,b,c,d\\}$};\n \\node[font=\\small] at (2.4,-1.25) {$t_1<t_2<t_3<t_4$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "On the left, a graph with four circular nodes labeled a, b, c, d. Solid edges connect node a to b (labeled $t_1$), node b to c (labeled $t_3$), and node c to d (labeled $t_2$). A dashed curved edge connects node a to d below the graph, labeled beneath with \"$t_4$: redundant\" and \"$t_1 < t_2 < t_3 < t_4$\". On the right, three set-merger relations: \"$t_1 : \\{a\\} \\cup \\{b\\} \\longrightarrow \\{a, b\\}$\", \"$t_2 : \\{c\\} \\cup \\{d\\} \\longrightarrow \\{c, d\\}$\", and \"$t_3 : \\{a, b\\} \\cup \\{c, d\\} \\longrightarrow \\{a, b, c, d\\}$\"."
    }
  ],

      "explanation": "The fluctuation analysis assumes the existence of a classical Boltzmann solution satisfying Gaussian velocity bounds on a finite time interval, which is established under the same initial regularity condition in the law of large numbers paper.",
